# Frontier LLM landscape

See [purpose](purpose.md) for the topic charter (in-scope / out-of-scope) and
`CLAUDE.md` "Cross-cutting relevance criteria" for the meta-bar.

## Summary

Anthropic's **Claude Opus 5** (shipped July 24, 2026) is the new **standard flagship** and **narrowly the most intelligent model in the world**: under the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index **v4.1.1** grader-robustness patch (2026-08-06), **Opus 5 (max) scores 63**, edging Claude Fable 5 (max, 62), GPT-5.6 Sol (max, 61) and Kimi K3 (57) [[launching-v4-1-1-of-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-8f7aaf85]] — a ~2-point top-cluster re-scale driven by grader improvements, **not a capability change**, so it leaves unchanged the ordering the 2026-07-26 primary evaluation first established (then 61/60/59/57 on v4.1) [[opus-5-fable-5-level-intelligence-at-a-lower-cost-per-task-864f9aa5]]. It is Anthropic's fourth model in under two months (after Fable 5 in early June and Sonnet 5 at end of June), replaces Opus 4.8 at **$5/$25 per Mtoken** (unchanged from Opus 4.8, vs Fable 5's $10/$50), and reaches Fable-5-class intelligence at **~26% lower cost per Intelligence Index task ($2.03 vs $2.75)** — so it now leads on **both aggregate intelligence and cost-per-task**, while also **topping Fable 5 on most per-benchmark axes at half the token price** [[introducing-claude-opus-5-c34a3276]] [[anthropic-launches-claude-opus-5-its-fourth-model-in-two-months-and-it-tops-fable-5-on-most-benchmarks-7253a489]] [[opus-5-fable-5-level-intelligence-at-a-lower-cost-per-task-864f9aa5]]. The aggregate-index #2 is Anthropic's Mythos-class **Claude Fable 5** at **62** (v4.1.1; was 60 on v4.1) [[launching-v4-1-1-of-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-8f7aaf85]], restored globally on July 1, 2026 after the US Commerce Department lifted its June 12–30 export-control suspension [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]] [[claude-opus-4-8-max-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-27b7d2eb]]. Redeployment did not roll back Fable 5's capabilities: Anthropic ships a new safety classifier that blocks the Amazon-reported vulnerability-discovery bypass in >99% of cases and reroutes flagged requests to Opus 4.8, and is proposing an industry-wide jailbreak-severity scoring framework with Amazon, Microsoft, Google and other Glasswing partners [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]] [[more-details-on-fable-5-s-cyber-safeguards-and-our-jailbreak-framework-aaef033d]]. The aggregate #3 slot is now a **two-way tie at 61** between **OpenAI's GPT-5.6 Sol (max)** (v4.1.1; was 59 on v4.1) [[launching-v4-1-1-of-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-8f7aaf85]] and SpaceXAI's new **Grok 4.6** (shipped Aug 12, 2026, +5 over Grok 4.5's 54) [[grok-4-6-returns-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-and-leads-on-cost-efficiency-bdc6284a]] — both ~2 points behind Opus 5 at a fraction of the flagship cost. GPT-5.6 Sol went GA July 9, 2026 and **leads the AA Coding Agent Index at 80**, so aggregate-intelligence leadership (Opus 5) and coding-agent leadership (GPT-5.6 Sol) have diverged [[gpt-5-6-benchmarks-across-intelligence-speed-and-cost-1b9cd635]]. **Grok 4.6** — a post-training upgrade over [[grok-4.5]] at **unchanged $2/$6 per Mtoken and a 500K-token context**, live day-one in Cursor — is the new **frontier token/turn cost-efficiency leader**, resolving tasks in **~half the turns and a quarter of the input tokens of Opus 5**, and posts the **#2 GDPval-AA v2 Elo (1753), second only to Opus 5**; it **supersedes Grok 4.5** as the SpaceXAI flagship [[grok-4-6-returns-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-and-leads-on-cost-efficiency-bdc6284a]] [[introducing-grok-4-6-66321e57]]. The highest-scoring model below the top-flagship cluster is Moonshot AI's **Kimi K3 at 57** — a 2.8-trillion-parameter, 1M-context Mixture-of-Experts model that entered on 2026-07-17 at **#4 overall** (behind Fable 5 = 60, GPT-5.6 Sol max = 59, and the Sol xhigh variant = 58) and, decisively, the **confirmed leading open-weights model**: its full 2.8T weights **landed on Hugging Face on July 27, 2026 as scheduled** under a Modified MIT license — released as an **MXFP4 (Microscaling FP4) quantization-aware-trained** checkpoint (~1.4TB, natively supported on NVIDIA Blackwell and AMD MI400) — making it the **leading open-weights model by a wide margin** over GLM-5.2 (51) [[kimi-k3-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-5d65f998]] [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]] [[kimi-k3-s-open-weights-arrive-july-27-the-catch-is-1-4tb-56e05201]]. Below it the cluster is tight (on v4.1.1 these lower models moved less than a point vs v4.1 [[launching-v4-1-1-of-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-8f7aaf85]]): **Claude Opus 4.8 (56)** — Anthropic's prior standard flagship, now superseded by Opus 5 at the same $5/$25 pricing — then **GPT-5.5 (55)**, **Claude Sonnet 5 (53)** (Grok 4.5's 54 is now superseded by Grok 4.6's 61), and the tied pair of **GLM-5.2 (51)** — now the **#2 open-weights model** behind Kimi K3 since the July 27 weights drop — and Meta's **Muse Spark 1.1 (51)**, with **Gemini 3.1 Pro at 46** [[claude-opus-4-8-max-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-27b7d2eb]] [[grok-4-5-brings-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-20480383]] [[claude-sonnet-5-strong-agentic-performance-at-a-higher-cost-per-task-c4346bb2]] [[glm-5-2-is-the-new-leading-open-weights-model-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-ef3ea3a0]] [[muse-spark-1-1-meta-gains-8-intelligence-index-points-in-three-months-56cc77a0]] [[gemini-3-1-pro-preview-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-3a3f9933]]. Fable 5 lists at $10/$50 per Mtoken — 2× Opus 5 / Opus 4.8 [[claude-fable-5-the-first-public-mythos-class-model-672c92f6]]; Kimi K3 lists at $3/$15 — 0.6× Opus 5 but 2.4× Muse Spark 1.1 — and its Mooncake disaggregated-inference stack drops the **cached** input price to **$0.30/MTok** [[kimi-k3-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-5d65f998]] [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]]; see [[claude-opus-5]], [[claude-fable-5]], [[claude-opus-4.8]], [[kimi-k3]], [[benchmark-leaders]] and [[cost-comparison]].

> Claude Opus 5 is our most intelligent model, delivering frontier performance for agentic coding, computer use, and complex reasoning at the same price as Claude Opus 4.8: $5 per million input tokens and $25 per million output tokens. [[introducing-claude-opus-5-c34a3276]]

> Opus 5 is the fourth model Anthropic has shipped in under two months, following Fable 5 in early June, Sonnet 5 at the end of June, and now Opus 5 on July 24. It costs $5/$25 per million tokens, the same price as Opus 4.8, and about half the price of Fable 5. Claude Opus 5 is narrowly the most intelligent model on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index, offering comparable intelligence to Fable 5 at 26% lower Cost per Task ($2.03 vs $2.75). [[opus-5-fable-5-level-intelligence-at-a-lower-cost-per-task-864f9aa5]]

GPT-5.6 Sol (max) leads the Artificial Analysis Coding Agent Index at 80 points. [[gpt-5-6-benchmarks-across-intelligence-speed-and-cost-1b9cd635]]

We are also proposing an industry-wide framework for scoring jailbreak severity, together with Amazon, Microsoft, Google, and other Glasswing partners. [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]]

MXFP4 is supported natively by NVIDIA Blackwell GPUs and AMD MI400 accelerators, making deployment hardware-friendly. [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]]

### Index basis: AA Intelligence Index v4.1.1 (read first)

The figures above are on **AA Intelligence Index v4.1.1**, the 2026-08-06 grader-robustness patch of the v4.1 agentic-workloads reweighting: v4.1.1 upgrades the graders for HLE, AA-LCR and AA-Omniscience to GPT-5.6 Luna (medium) and moves τ³-Banking to v1.0.1, lifting the top cluster ~2 points (Opus 5 61→63, Fable 5 60→62, GPT-5.6 Sol 59→61) while leaving ordering unchanged — a grading-robustness re-baseline, not a capability move, so **v4.1 → v4.1.1 numbers are again not strictly like-for-like** [[launching-v4-1-1-of-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-8f7aaf85]]. v4.1 itself was the agentic-workloads reweighting Artificial Analysis migrated to on 2026-06-17 that **re-scales every model's score** — v4.0 and v4.1 numbers are **not directly comparable** [[artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-v4-1-a-shift-toward-agentic-workloads-e0bce552]]. v4.1 is a weighted average across four categories — **Agents 34% / Coding 24% / Scientific Reasoning 24% / General 18%** — so agentic + coding tasks are now 58% of the aggregate, making it materially more representative of agentic-coding workflows than v4.0 [[claude-opus-4-8-max-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-27b7d2eb]]. It reweights evaluations toward agentic tasks (Terminal-Bench Hard → 2.1, τ²-Bench Telecom → τ³-Bench Banking), upgrades GDPval-AA to **GDPval-AA v2** (now the single highest-weighted evaluation, with Elo re-baselined to human-expert = 1000, a rotating frontier-model judge panel, and the agent turn limit raised 100 → 250), and drops IFBench for saturation [[artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-v4-1-a-shift-toward-agentic-workloads-e0bce552]]. The re-scale is large — e.g. Kimi K2.6 moves 54 (v4.0) → 43 (v4.1) and GLM-5.1 51 (v4.0) → 40 (v4.1) [[glm-5-2-is-the-new-leading-open-weights-model-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-ef3ea3a0]]. All major frontier models are now v4.1.1-placed: **Claude Opus 5 (max, 63 — #1)** [[launching-v4-1-1-of-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-8f7aaf85]], Fable 5 (62, restored July 1), the tied #3 pair **GPT-5.6 Sol (61)** and **Grok 4.6 (61)** [[grok-4-6-returns-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-and-leads-on-cost-efficiency-bdc6284a]], Kimi K3 (57), Opus 4.8 (56), GPT-5.5 (55), Sonnet 5 (53), Qwen3.8 Max (53), Gemini 3.1 Pro (46) — and Grok 4.3's v4.1 = 38 was superseded by Grok 4.5 (54), now itself superseded by **Grok 4.6 (61)** [[grok-4-3-high-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-18472372]] [[grok-4-5-brings-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-20480383]] [[kimi-k3-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-5d65f998]]. The last placement pending — **Claude Opus 5** (shipped July 24) — was resolved by Artificial Analysis's 2026-07-26 primary evaluation at **61 (max)** on v4.1 — since re-scaled to **63** on the 2026-08-06 v4.1.1 grader patch — making it the aggregate-index #1 and completing the migration for all tracked flagships [[opus-5-fable-5-level-intelligence-at-a-lower-cost-per-task-864f9aa5]] [[launching-v4-1-1-of-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-8f7aaf85]]. The v4.0 era is fully superseded for these models; any lesser-tracked model still carrying a v4.0 score is explicitly tagged.

> Claude Opus 5 remains in the #1 position with an Index of 63. Overall model rankings remain largely consistent, with a slight increase in scores due to improved grading robustness. [[launching-v4-1-1-of-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-8f7aaf85]]

> Intelligence Index v4.1 is calculated as a weighted average across four categories: Agents (34%), Coding (24%), Scientific Reasoning (24%) and General (18%), with the weighting emphasizing agentic tasks.

> v4.1 marks a broader shift toward agentic workloads ... upgrading GDPval-AA to GDPval-AA v2; and removing IFBench due to saturation. GDPval-AA v2 is the highest weighted evaluation in the Intelligence Index v4.1.

### Agentic and coding leadership

The July 24 Opus 5 launch reshuffles per-benchmark leadership: **Opus 5 leads Frontier-Bench at 43.3% (max effort)** — above Fable 5's 33.7% and GPT-5.6 Sol's 37.5% — posts a verified **30.16% on ARC-AGI-3** (~3× the next model), **70.57% on OSWorld 2.0** (vs Opus 4.8's 55.7%, making it the strongest computer-use/browser-agent model tested), and **26.0% on Zapier AutomationBench** (vs Opus 4.8 17.0% and Fable 5 17.4%) [[meet-the-new-claude-opus-5-frontier-class-agentic-coding-and-computer-use-at-unchanged-opus-pricing-7f7892b3]] [[anthropic-launches-claude-opus-5-its-fourth-model-in-two-months-and-it-tops-fable-5-on-most-benchmarks-7253a489]]. On the contamination-resistant **SWE-bench Pro** axis this page treats as the credible frontier-coding signal, **Claude Fable 5 still leads at 80.0%**, edging Opus 5 and remaining Anthropic's recommendation for the most advanced multi-day autonomous agent work — more than 20 points ahead of GPT-5.5 (58.6%) and Gemini 3.1 Pro (54.2%) [[meet-the-new-claude-opus-5-frontier-class-agentic-coding-and-computer-use-at-unchanged-opus-pricing-7f7892b3]] [[claude-fable-5-the-first-public-mythos-class-model-672c92f6]] [[claude-opus-4-8-benchmarks-explained-60247f20]]. On **GDPval-AA v2** — the highest-weighted v4.1 evaluation, re-baselined to human-expert = 1000 and not comparable to the retired v1 Elo — **Claude Opus 5 (max) now leads at the highest recorded 1861 Elo**, with **SpaceXAI's Grok 4.6 second at 1753 — behind only Opus 5, its confidence intervals overlapping Fable 5 and Qwen3.8 Max** [[grok-4-6-returns-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-and-leads-on-cost-efficiency-bdc6284a]]; among earlier-measured models Fable 5 (with fallback) posted 1818, above Opus 4.8's 1638 and GPT-5.5 (xhigh) at 1531 [[claude-opus-5-the-new-leader-in-agentic-knowledge-work-92cf92da]] [[claude-opus-4-8-max-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-27b7d2eb]]. On the AA **Coding Agent Index**, leadership diverges from the aggregate index: **GPT-5.6 Sol (max) leads at 80** [[gpt-5-6-benchmarks-across-intelligence-speed-and-cost-1b9cd635]]. On the contamination-audited math axis, Fable 5 also leads FrontierMath (88% on the hardest Tier 4 v2, ~13 points over GPT-5.5) — though that audit corrected errors in 42% of problems, a benchmark-integrity caution [[claude-fable-5-outpaces-gpt-5-5-by-13-points-on-frontiermath-s-toughest-problems-b1737496]] [[frontiermath-tier-4-v2-9558ff2f]]. On **AA-Briefcase** — the frontier knowledge-work evaluation — **Claude Opus 5 (max) now leads at Elo 1720 (+146 over Fable 5)**, ahead of Fable 5 (1574) and Kimi K3 (1543) [[claude-opus-5-the-new-leader-in-agentic-knowledge-work-92cf92da]] [[kimi-k3-second-only-to-fable-5-on-aa-briefcase-a126a86d]]. Below Opus 5 the sub-axes still split: Kimi K3 edges Fable 5 on **analytical quality (1754 vs 1744 Elo)** while trailing on presentation (1471) and rubric pass rate (51% vs 56%) — so on pure analytical depth the aggregate-index #4 (Kimi K3, 57) can top Fable 5 (62) [[kimi-k3-second-only-to-fable-5-on-aa-briefcase-a126a86d]].

> Opus 5 reached 70.57% on OSWorld 2.0 against 55.7% for Opus 4.8. On Zapier AutomationBench it scored 26.0%, against 17.0% for Opus 4.8 and 17.4% for Fable 5. Fable 5 still edges Opus 5 on SWE-bench Pro at 80.0%. [[meet-the-new-claude-opus-5-frontier-class-agentic-coding-and-computer-use-at-unchanged-opus-pricing-7f7892b3]]

> Claude Opus 5 tops Fable 5 on most benchmarks at half the token price and is Anthropic's most aligned model to date. [[anthropic-launches-claude-opus-5-its-fourth-model-in-two-months-and-it-tops-fable-5-on-most-benchmarks-7253a489]]

> On SWE-bench Pro, Fable 5 scores 80.3%, an 11-point lead over Opus 4.8 (69.2%) and more than 20 points ahead of GPT-5.5 (58.6%) and Gemini 3.1 Pro (54.2%).

> According to Epoch AI, Fable 5 hits 87 percent accuracy on tiers 1 through 3 and 88 percent on the hardest tier 4 (v2) ... approximately a 13-point difference in performance on FrontierMath's toughest problems.

### Per-lab flagships

**Anthropic**: the standard flagship is now **Claude Opus 5** (shipped July 24, 2026, v4.1.1 AA Index **63 — the aggregate-index #1**, $5/$25 per Mtoken, 1M-token context, research-preview Fast mode ~2.5× faster at $10/$50), which tops Fable 5 on most benchmarks at half the token price, leads Frontier-Bench, ARC-AGI-3, OSWorld 2.0, Zapier AutomationBench, **GDPval-AA v2 (1861)** and **AA-Briefcase (1720)**, and reaches Fable-5-class intelligence at **~26% lower cost per task ($2.03 vs $2.75)**; it **supersedes Opus 4.8** (v4.1 AA Index 56) at the same price [[introducing-claude-opus-5-c34a3276]] [[meet-the-new-claude-opus-5-frontier-class-agentic-coding-and-computer-use-at-unchanged-opus-pricing-7f7892b3]] [[opus-5-fable-5-level-intelligence-at-a-lower-cost-per-task-864f9aa5]] [[claude-opus-5-the-new-leader-in-agentic-knowledge-work-92cf92da]]. The Mythos-class **Fable 5** (v4.1.1 AA Index 62, aggregate-index #2, restored July 1, $10/$50 per Mtoken) still leads SWE-bench Pro (80.0%) and remains Anthropic's recommendation for the most advanced multi-day autonomous agent work [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]] [[meet-the-new-claude-opus-5-frontier-class-agentic-coding-and-computer-use-at-unchanged-opus-pricing-7f7892b3]]; the new agentic mid-tier is **Sonnet 5** (v4.1 AA Index 53; $2/$10 introductory through Aug 31, 2026, reverting to $3/$15) [[claude-sonnet-5-strong-agentic-performance-at-a-higher-cost-per-task-c4346bb2]]; the original **Claude Mythos Preview** remains the internal capability ceiling, state-of-the-art on SWE-bench Verified (93.9%) and Terminal-Bench 2.0 (82.0%) [[claude-mythos-preview-d737ab91]]; see [[claude-opus-5]], [[claude-fable-5]], [[claude-sonnet-5]] and [[claude-mythos]]. **OpenAI**'s flagship is **GPT-5.6 Sol** (v4.1.1 AA Index 61, aggregate #3; retires GPT-5.5 as flagship), which leads the AA Coding Agent Index at 80 and ships an `ultra` mode coordinating four agents in parallel by default, at $5/$30 per Mtoken; Terra (55) and Luna (51) fill the balanced/cost tiers [[gpt-5-6-frontier-intelligence-that-scales-with-your-ambition-e19fbb75]] [[gpt-5-6-benchmarks-across-intelligence-speed-and-cost-1b9cd635]]; see [[gpt-5.6-sol]] and [[gpt-5.5]]. **Google**'s public frontier is **Gemini 3.1 Pro** (v4.1 AA Index 46 [[gemini-3-1-pro-preview-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-3a3f9933]]); the Flash-tier workhorse is now **Gemini 3.6 Flash** (shipped July 22, 2026, AA Intelligence Index 50 at $1.50/$7.50 per Mtoken), which replaces Gemini 3.5 Flash at the same index score but halves time per task, generates 17% fewer output tokens, reaches 275 tok/sec, and cuts cost-per-task ~18% [[introducing-gemini-3-6-flash-3-5-flash-lite-and-3-5-flash-cyber-268e6f2a]] [[gemini-3-6-flash-and-gemini-3-5-flash-lite-halving-time-per-task-e581ad77]]; the cost-efficient tier is **Gemini 3.5 Flash-Lite** (AA Intelligence Index 36), and Google also shipped a cybersecurity-specialized **Gemini 3.5 Flash Cyber** variant [[gemini-3-6-flash-and-gemini-3-5-flash-lite-halving-time-per-task-e581ad77]]. The **3.5 Pro** frontier tier remains pre-GA (limited preview into July) [[gemini-3-5-frontier-intelligence-with-action-f4ceaac7]], targeting a 2M-token context and Deep Think reasoning at ~$15/$60 per Mtoken [[google-gemini-3-5-pro-nears-june-launch-with-2-million-token-context-and-deep-think-reasoning-3a0c8ff3]]; see [[gemini-3.1-pro]]. **xAI/SpaceXAI**'s flagship is now **Grok 4.6** (shipped Aug 12, 2026; v4.1.1 AA Index **61**, +5 over Grok 4.5's 54 and +23 over Grok 4.3, tying GPT-5.6 Sol for aggregate #3), a post-training upgrade on the Grok 4.5 foundation at **unchanged $2/$6 per Mtoken** with a **500K-token context**, live day-one in Cursor and Grok Build plus the API, OpenRouter, Vercel and Cloudflare; it is the **frontier token/turn cost-efficiency leader** (~53 turns / ~0.5B input tokens per task vs ~103 turns / ~2.0B for Opus 5 max) and posts **GDPval-AA v2 Elo 1753 (second only to Opus 5)**, AA-Briefcase Elo 1577, τ³-Banking 50.7% (top two) and Terminal-Bench v2.1 88.4% (level with the leaders) [[introducing-grok-4-6-66321e57]] [[grok-4-6-returns-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-and-leads-on-cost-efficiency-bdc6284a]]. It **supersedes Grok 4.5** (v4.1 AA Index 54, trained alongside Cursor), which in Grok Build was the cheapest-per-task coding agent tested at $2.49/task (AA Coding Agent Index 76) [[introducing-grok-4-5-c5fe4e15]] [[grok-4-5-brings-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-20480383]]; the model-routing-neutrality concern raised by SpaceX's **$60B agreement to acquire Anysphere (Cursor)** deepens with Grok 4.6's day-one Cursor launch [[grok-v9-medium-arrives-as-spacex-seals-cursor-developers-face-model-choice-risk-37bb8bd7]]; see [[grok-4.6]], [[grok-4.5]]. **Meta** re-enters the frontier board with **Muse Spark 1.2** (released August 5, 2026) behind the Meta Model API — v4.1 AA Index 54 (xhigh), up 3 points from Muse Spark 1.1's 51 and 11 from Muse Spark 1.0's 43, effectively tied with Grok 4.5 (54) and one point behind GPT-5.5 (55); it pairs a 1M-token context, native multimodality, built-in cited search and parallel tool calling with aggressive $1.25/$4.25 per Mtoken pricing (~1/4 of Anthropic/OpenAI flagship rates) [[muse-spark-1-2-3d7d1796]]; see [[muse-spark]]. **Microsoft** entered the frontier tier with its first in-house reasoning model, **MAI-Thinking-1** (35B-active sparse MoE, 256K context), claiming Opus-4.6-class coding [[introducing-mai-thinking-1-0e3e1662]]. **Moonshot AI**'s flagship is **Kimi K3** (v4.1.1 AA Index 57, #4 overall) — a 2.8-trillion-parameter, 1M-context Mixture-of-Experts model built for frontier software engineering, knowledge work and deep reasoning at $3/$15 per Mtoken ($0.30/MTok cached); it is now the **confirmed leading open-weights model**, its full 2.8T weights having **landed on Hugging Face on July 27, 2026** under a Modified MIT license as an MXFP4 quantization-aware-trained checkpoint, extending Moonshot's open Kimi K2.6 (43) line to a far higher tier [[kimi-k3-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-5d65f998]] [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]] [[kimi-k3-s-open-weights-arrive-july-27-the-catch-is-1-4tb-56e05201]]; see [[kimi-k3]]. **Alibaba**'s flagship is **Qwen3.8 Max** (v4.1 AA Index 53, superseding the Qwen3.7 Max line) — a 2.4T-total / ~95B-active Mixture-of-Experts model with a 1M-token context and text/image/video input, priced at $2/$6 per Mtoken (blended ~$1.18/Mtok, cheaper than Kimi K3's $3/$15 and roughly matching Grok 4.5's $2/$6) — which **ties Claude Sonnet 5 (53)** and sits just below **Grok 4.5 (54)**, so it does not move the unchanged top cluster (Opus 5 61 / Fable 5 60 / GPT-5.6 Sol 59 / Kimi K3 57); it is **proprietary at launch** (served via Alibaba Cloud), with a floated open-weights release plus a smaller 27B variant remaining unconfirmed, and AA flags it as "notably slow and very verbose" at just 46.5 tok/sec — a real cost/latency multiplier on output-dominated agentic loops [[qwen3-8-max-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-0ad85146]]; see [[qwen3.7-max]].

### Open-weights leaders

On the **new v4.1 basis**, open-weights leadership has now changed hands: Moonshot AI's **Kimi K3 (v4.1 = 57)** **released its full 2.8-trillion-parameter weights on Hugging Face on July 27, 2026 as scheduled**, under a **Modified MIT license** (the same license as K2.6 and K2.7-Code) — making it the **confirmed leading open-weights model by a wide margin** — Moonshot's first-party tech blog calls it "the world's first open 3T-class model" — over the now-#2 **GLM-5.2 (51)** and DeepSeek V4-Pro (44) [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]] [[kimi-k3-tech-blog-open-frontier-intelligence-0d83e2aa]] [[glm-5-2-is-the-new-leading-open-weights-model-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-ef3ea3a0]]. The drop shipped in **MXFP4 (Microscaling FP4)** applied via **quantization-aware training from the SFT stage** (not post-training quantization), a first-party claim of substantially lower quality degradation than standard PTQ; MXFP4 is natively supported on NVIDIA Blackwell and AMD MI400, and on task coverage **K3 leads all models on SWE Marathon and Program Bench**, consistent with its 1M-context advantage on sustained coding sessions [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]]. Architecturally K3 is a **Mixture-of-Experts** model activating **16 of 896 experts (~50B active params)** per token via a Stable LatentMoE framework, built on **Kimi Delta Attention** (a gated delta-rule linear-attention variant using channel-wise rather than scalar forgetting) and **Attention Residuals**, for ~2.5x better scaling efficiency than Kimi K2 [[kimi-k3-tech-blog-open-frontier-intelligence-0d83e2aa]]. The open-weights win still carries a hard self-hosting barrier: the weights total **~1.4TB (≈594GB in BF16**; community GGUF Q4 ~300–400GB), so K3 is practically self-hostable only on multi-node H100/B200 clusters, out of reach for smaller shops [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]] [[kimi-k3-s-open-weights-arrive-july-27-the-catch-is-1-4tb-56e05201]]. Nathan Lambert calls K3 "the strongest open model ever released" and reports Alibaba has queued a 2.4-trillion-parameter Qwen 3.8 open-weights model, signaling an escalating open-weights race at the 2–3T scale [[kimi-k3-the-open-weights-escalation-be621fe3]]. The **#2 open-weights model** is now Z.ai's **GLM-5.2** at **AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 51** — 11 points above GLM-5.1's v4.1 score — ahead of MiniMax-M3 (44), DeepSeek V4-Pro max (44) and Kimi K2.6 (43) [[glm-5-2-is-the-new-leading-open-weights-model-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-ef3ea3a0]]. GLM-5.2 keeps GLM-5's 744B-total / 40B-active MoE size, adds a usable **1M-token context** (among the largest from any open-weights LLM), and prices at $1.4/$4.4/$0.26 per 1M input/output/cache-hit tokens; its gains concentrate in scientific reasoning, long-context reasoning (AA-LCR +9 to 71%) and agentic banking [[zhipu-ai-open-sources-glm-5-2-with-1-million-token-context-cbc95c1c]] [[glm-5-2-is-the-new-leading-open-weights-model-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-ef3ea3a0]]; see [[glm-5]]. This **supersedes** the prior open-weights framing (MiniMax-M3 55 / Kimi-K2.6 54 / MiMo-V2.5-Pro 54 / DeepSeek V4-Pro 52), which was measured on the now-retired **v4.0** index [[minimax-m3-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-418bc9a9]] [[mimo-v2-5-pro-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-51e3baae]]. Verbosity is the persistent caveat: GLM-5.2 burns ~43k output tokens per index task (37k reasoning), well above MiniMax-M3's 24k — a real cost multiplier on output-dominated agentic loops [[glm-5-2-is-the-new-leading-open-weights-model-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-ef3ea3a0]]. Other open-weights anchors: **MiniMax M3** remains the first open-weight model combining frontier-tier coding, a 1M-token context and native multimodality at ~5-10% of closed-frontier prices [[minimax-m3-frontier-coding-1m-context-native-multimodality-all-in-one-model-d466ccc6]]; see [[minimax-m3]]. **DeepSeek V4-Pro** anchors the top tier (see [[deepseek-v4]]); see also [[kimi-k2.6]]. Among US/non-Chinese open weights, NVIDIA's **Nemotron 3 Ultra** leads (v4.0 AA Index 48), distinguished by 300+ tokens/sec throughput [[nemotron-3-ultra-launches-june-4-the-first-open-frontier-model-built-for-agents-302459f4]].

> Kimi K3 is the world's first open 3T-class model — frontier performance across coding, knowledge work, and reasoning, with native multimodality and 1M context. [[kimi-k3-tech-blog-open-frontier-intelligence-0d83e2aa]]

> Kimi K3 has scaled up Mixture of Experts (MoE) sparsity, effectively activating 16 out of 896 experts when paired with a Stable LatentMoE framework ... an approximate 2.5x improvement in overall scaling efficiency compared to Kimi K2. [[kimi-k3-tech-blog-open-frontier-intelligence-0d83e2aa]]

> [Kimi K3 is] the strongest open model ever released ... a watershed moment because frontier open-weight models are now real. [[kimi-k3-the-open-weights-escalation-be621fe3]]

> K3 leads all models on SWE Marathon and Program Bench, suggesting particular strength in sustained coding sessions — consistent with the 1M-token context window enabling full-repository understanding. [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]]

> Z.ai's GLM-5.2 is the new leading open weights model on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index, scoring 51. GLM-5.2 is the same size as GLM-5.1 (744B total / 40B active parameters) but scores 11 points higher on the Intelligence Index v4.1, placing ahead of MiniMax-M3 (44), DeepSeek V4 Pro (max, 44) and Kimi K2.6 (43).

> GLM-5.2 ... with a usable 1-million-token context window and a new dual thinking-effort system (High and Max).

> MiniMax-M3 (Reasoning) achieves a score of 55 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index, placing it well above average among comparable models (averaging 23).

> M3 is currently the first and only open-weight model to bring all three together (frontier coding capabilities, 1M context, and native multimodality).

> Nemotron 3 Ultra ranks as the #1 US open-weights model with an Intelligence Index of 48, delivering 300+ tokens/second. The model offers up to 5x faster inference and up to 30% lower cost than open frontier models in its class.

### Architecture and efficiency stories

The most consequential architectural entrant is **SubQ** (Subquadratic), the first LLM on a fully subquadratic (linear-scaling) attention architecture with a **12M-token context window**, reporting ~1,000x less attention compute at 12M tokens — though every headline number is vendor-run and not yet independently reproduced [[the-context-window-has-been-shattered-subquadratic-debuts-a-12-million-token-window-badc2592]]. On cost-efficiency, Cursor's **Composer 2.5** ranks third on the AA Coding Agent Index while costing ~10-60x less per task than the Claude Code / Codex leaders — reshaping the economics of multi-agent fan-out [[cursor-s-composer-2-5-third-on-the-coding-agent-index-b9ec8ce6]].

> The SSA architecture scales linearly with context length instead of quadratically, cutting attention compute by roughly 1,000x at 12M tokens.

> These competitors cost $4.10 and $4.82 per task respectively, ~10x the cost of Composer 2.5 Fast ($0.44) and ~60x the cost of Composer 2.5 standard ($0.07).

### Takeaways for an agentic-coding + game-dev workflow

For a quality-first agentic-coding loop the default is now unambiguous: **Claude Opus 5** is the **outright default flagship** — it is **#1 on aggregate intelligence (v4.1.1 = 63; 61 on v4.1)** [[launching-v4-1-1-of-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-8f7aaf85]], leads GDPval-AA v2 (1861) and AA-Briefcase (1720), tops Fable 5 on most per-benchmark axes (Frontier-Bench, ARC-AGI-3, OSWorld 2.0, Zapier AutomationBench) and leads computer-use on OSWorld 2.0 (70.57%), all at **half the token price ($5/$25)** and **~26% lower cost per task ($2.03 vs $2.75)** [[introducing-claude-opus-5-c34a3276]] [[meet-the-new-claude-opus-5-frontier-class-agentic-coding-and-computer-use-at-unchanged-opus-pricing-7f7892b3]] [[opus-5-fable-5-level-intelligence-at-a-lower-cost-per-task-864f9aa5]] [[claude-opus-5-the-new-leader-in-agentic-knowledge-work-92cf92da]] — while **Fable 5** ($10/$50) retains only the **top-end long-horizon-coding crown** (still leads SWE-bench Pro at 80.0% and remains Anthropic's recommendation for the most advanced multi-day autonomous agent work), and **GPT-5.6 Sol** leads the AA Coding Agent Index (80): reach for Opus 5 by default, Fable 5 for top-end long-horizon coding, and Sol when coding-agent throughput and its `ultra` parallel-agent mode matter [[anthropic-launches-claude-opus-5-its-fourth-model-in-two-months-and-it-tops-fable-5-on-most-benchmarks-7253a489]] [[claude-fable-5-the-first-public-mythos-class-model-672c92f6]] [[gpt-5-6-benchmarks-across-intelligence-speed-and-cost-1b9cd635]]. For cost-sensitive parallel-subagent fan-out (and high-iteration game-dev content/tooling pipelines), the new default is **Grok 4.6** — the **frontier token/turn cost-efficiency leader** at $2/$6 per Mtoken (500K context) that reaches comparable answers in ~half the turns and a quarter of the input tokens of Opus 5 while scoring aggregate-#3-tie intelligence (61), a far bigger cost win than its 60%+ per-token discount alone [[grok-4-6-returns-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-and-leads-on-cost-efficiency-bdc6284a]]; below it, the superseded **Grok 4.5** in Grok Build ($2.49/task) [[grok-4-5-brings-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-20480383]], Cursor's **Composer 2.5** [[cursor-s-composer-2-5-third-on-the-coding-agent-index-b9ec8ce6]], Meta's **Muse Spark 1.1** ($1.25/$4.25, 1M-context multimodal, ~$0.26/task) [[muse-spark-1-1-meta-gains-8-intelligence-index-points-in-three-months-56cc77a0]], or an open-weight 1M-context model like **GLM-5.2** (verbose) or **MiniMax M3** change the per-task economics dramatically [[glm-5-2-is-the-new-leading-open-weights-model-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-ef3ea3a0]] [[minimax-m3-frontier-coding-1m-context-native-multimodality-all-in-one-model-d466ccc6]]. For shops that can host it, the now-live open-weights leader **Kimi K3** (v4.1.1 = 57, MXFP4, $0.30/MTok cached, leads SWE Marathon and Program Bench) is the strongest fully-downloadable option — but its ~1.4TB footprint and ~130M-tokens/task verbosity confine it to multi-node H100/B200 clusters [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]].

### Key open caveats

The public leaderboard is now shaped by regulation and methodology, not just capability. Anthropic's Mythos-class Fable 5 was **export-suspended June 12–30** then restored July 1 via a request-routing safety classifier (flagged prompts fall back to Opus 4.8) rather than a capability rollback [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]] [[commerce-department-gives-green-light-for-anthropic-to-bring-back-fable-5-e657d00f]]; the higher Mythos 5 and the original Mythos Preview remain gated [[anthropic-confirms-claude-mythos-class-models-will-roll-out-to-the-public-11ba8929]] — so the public board still is not the true ceiling. **Claude Opus 5's aggregate placement is now primary-confirmed**: Artificial Analysis's 2026-07-26 evaluation put Opus 5 (max) at **61 on the AA Intelligence Index v4.1 — the aggregate-index #1** (since re-scaled to **63** on the 2026-08-06 v4.1.1 grader patch) [[launching-v4-1-1-of-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-8f7aaf85]] — retiring the prior ~61 re-aggregator estimate that had lacked an artificialanalysis.ai source; Anthropic's own launch materials remain per-benchmark-only (Frontier-Bench 43.3%, ARC-AGI-3 30.16%, OSWorld 2.0 70.57%, Zapier AutomationBench 26.0%) [[opus-5-fable-5-level-intelligence-at-a-lower-cost-per-task-864f9aa5]] [[meet-the-new-claude-opus-5-frontier-class-agentic-coding-and-computer-use-at-unchanged-opus-pricing-7f7892b3]]. The **v4.0 → v4.1 → v4.1.1 index re-scales** mean cross-version AA Intelligence Index comparisons are invalid; on the current **v4.1.1** basis (2026-08-06 grader patch) the top cluster is Opus 5 = 63, Fable 5 = 62, GPT-5.6 Sol = 61, Grok 4.6 = 61, Kimi K3 = 57, with the lower cluster essentially unchanged from v4.1 (Opus 4.8 = 56, GPT-5.5 = 55, Sonnet 5 = 53, Qwen3.8 Max = 53, Gemini 3.1 Pro = 46, GLM-5.2 = 51; Grok 4.5's 54 is superseded by Grok 4.6's 61; AA reports most models moved less than a point) — the ~2-point top-three lift is a grading-robustness artifact, not a capability move [[artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-v4-1-a-shift-toward-agentic-workloads-e0bce552]] [[launching-v4-1-1-of-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-8f7aaf85]] [[opus-5-fable-5-level-intelligence-at-a-lower-cost-per-task-864f9aa5]] [[gemini-3-1-pro-preview-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-3a3f9933]] [[kimi-k3-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-5d65f998]]. New entrants carry cost-of-verbosity and self-hosting caveats: **Kimi K3 (57)** places #4 overall but burns ~130M tokens per index task (>2× the 63M average) and runs slower than average (62 vs 70 tok/sec) — real cost/latency multipliers on output-dominated agentic loops that partly offset its sub-Opus $3/$15 pricing ($0.30/MTok cached) [[kimi-k3-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-5d65f998]]; and although its full 2.8T weights are **now open (landed July 27, 2026 as an MXFP4 QAT release)**, the **~1.4TB (≈594GB BF16)** footprint still confines practical self-hosting to multi-node H100/B200 clusters, out of reach for smaller shops absent GGUF Q4 (~300–400GB) quantization [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]] [[kimi-k3-s-open-weights-arrive-july-27-the-catch-is-1-4tb-56e05201]]. The same pattern shows on AA-Briefcase, where Kimi K3 (analytical-quality leader at 1754 Elo) costs **$10.57 per task, averages 83 turns (vs 67 for Fable 5), and takes 56.4 minutes** — among the most expensive models to run on the workload despite its per-Mtoken price advantage, so its analytical edge does not translate into a cheaper knowledge-work agent [[kimi-k3-second-only-to-fable-5-on-aa-briefcase-a126a86d]]. Benchmark integrity remains contested — SWE-bench Verified is suspected of contamination, so SWE-bench Pro is the more credible cross-model signal [[claude-opus-4-8-benchmarks-explained-60247f20]], and the FrontierMath audit corrected 42% of problems [[frontiermath-tier-4-v2-9558ff2f]]. Vendor-reported leadership is not yet independently corroborated for several entrants: Muse Spark 1.1's Meta-reported tool-use SOTA (MCP Atlas 88.1, JobBench 54.7) sits mid-cluster on the independent aggregate index and trails on contamination-resistant coding (SWE-Bench Pro 61.5 vs Opus 4.8's 69.2) [[introducing-muse-spark-1-1-f8a95609]] [[muse-spark-1-1-meta-gains-8-intelligence-index-points-in-three-months-56cc77a0]]; Sakana's Fugu-Ultra orchestrator reports state-of-the-art SWE-bench Pro but as a vendor-run multi-agent system, not a single model [[sakana-fugu-technical-report-a92f171d]]; and SubQ's 12M-token subquadratic claims remain unreproduced. A structural watch item: xAI's Grok 4.5/4.6 line was co-trained with Cursor — and Grok 4.6 launched day-one in Cursor — as SpaceX moves to acquire Anysphere/Cursor, a model-routing-neutrality risk for the multi-model IDE [[grok-v9-medium-arrives-as-spacex-seals-cursor-developers-face-model-choice-risk-37bb8bd7]] [[introducing-grok-4-6-66321e57]]. Grok 4.6's contamination-resistant coding placement (SWE-bench Pro) was not reported at launch [[grok-4-6-returns-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-and-leads-on-cost-efficiency-bdc6284a]]. Several entrants (SubQ, MiniMax M3, MAI-Thinking-1, Sakana Fugu) remain without independent placement (see `## Disputes` and `## Open questions`).

## Recent updates

### Updates 2026-08-17

**Daily sweep (2026-08-17) — one qualifying source, no leader changes.** Alibaba's Qwen team published the model card for **Qwen3.8-27B**, a compact, deployment-friendly **27B dense vision-language model** (Apache 2.0) with flexible thinking control, a **262,144-token native context** (1M planned via the hosted API), and a **3:1 hybrid of Gated DeltaNet linear attention and gated full attention** (24Q/4KV heads, head dim 256, vocab 248,320) [[qwen3-8-27b-compact-open-weight-dense-vlm-hugging-face-ca26bca1]]. The vendor-reported benchmarks are notable for a 27B model: **Terminal-Bench 2.1 73.0%**, **SWE-bench Pro 61.7%** (just under Claude Sonnet 5's 63.2%), **OSWorld-Verified 84.3%** (above Claude Sonnet 5's 81.2% on the same benchmark variant), **AndroidWorld 81.9%**, and **LiveCodeBench v6 90.3%** — claiming parity with or better than Muse Glimmer-30B and Claude Opus 4.6 Max on several axes. No **AA Intelligence Index** placement was published at model-card release, so independent aggregate-intelligence placement is pending. The overall frontier leader picture is unchanged: **Claude Opus 5 (max, 63)** remains #1, followed by Fable 5 (62), Grok 4.6 and GPT-5.6 Sol tied at #3 (61), and Kimi K3 (57) as the open-weights leader.

> Qwen3.8-27B often rivals or exceeds larger models like Muse Glimmer-30B and even Opus 4.6 Max on several benchmarks — scoring 73.0 on Terminal-Bench 2.1, 61.7 on SWE-bench Pro, 84.3 on OSWorld-Verified computer-use, 81.9 on AndroidWorld mobile-use, and 90.3 on LiveCodeBench v6. [[qwen3-8-27b-compact-open-weight-dense-vlm-hugging-face-ca26bca1]]

> On SWE-bench Pro, Qwen hits 61.7% vs Claude Sonnet 5 at 63.2%, and on OSWorld-Verified, Qwen hits 84.3% vs Claude Sonnet 5 at 81.2%. [[qwen3-8-27b-compact-open-weight-dense-vlm-hugging-face-ca26bca1]]

### Updates 2026-08-14

**Two sub-frontier releases land — DeepSeek-V4-Pro-0813 GA (Aug 13) and Upstage Solar Pro 4 (Aug 12) — neither of which moves the leader picture; the open-weights leader remains Kimi K3 (57).** DeepSeek shipped the **GA build of V4-Pro (`DeepSeek-V4-Pro-0813`)** on 2026-08-13, superseding the April preview. It is a **1.57T-total / 48B-active MoE** built on the V4-Pro preview structure **with a DSpark speculative-decoding module attached**, and the GA build "greatly enhances agent capabilities" with "particularly significant performance improvements in production environments"; V4-Pro and V4-Flash thinking now expose **three effort levels (low/high/max)** [[deepseek-v4-pro-ga-release-fe54be56]]. Artificial Analysis re-places the 0813 build at **53 on the Intelligence Index v4.1.1** — up 1 point from the V4-Pro preview's 52 on v4.0, but still **below open-weights leader Kimi K3 (57)** and well below the closed frontier (Opus 5 max 63 / Fable 5 62 / GPT-5.6 Sol ≈ Grok 4.6 61), so it **does not move the frontier or open-weights leader** [[deepseek-v4-pro-intelligence-and-performance-analysis-artificial-analysis-36762786]]. **New API pricing takes effect 16:00 UTC 2026-08-16** (rates not published in the GA note), so the entity's current cost fields may move on the next sweep [[deepseek-v4-pro-ga-release-fe54be56]].

> DeepSeek-V4-Pro-0813 is a 1.57T parameter model with 48B active parameters per token. It is built on the DeepSeek-V4-Pro (Preview) model structure, with a DSpark speculative decoding module attached. [[deepseek-v4-pro-ga-release-fe54be56]]

> DeepSeek V4 Pro 0813 (Reasoning, Max Effort) scores 53 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index, placing it well above average among comparable models (median: 27). [[deepseek-v4-pro-intelligence-and-performance-analysis-artificial-analysis-36762786]]

Upstage separately shipped **Solar Pro 4** (Aug 12), its new proprietary flagship reasoning model replacing Solar Pro 3 (April). Artificial Analysis places it at **42 on the Intelligence Index** — tied with Inkling (xhigh, 42), just behind MiMo-V2.5-Pro (43), and a **+27-point jump over Solar Pro 3** concentrated in agentic/long-context work (Terminal-Bench v2.1 12%→57%, AA-LCR 31%→71%, τ³-Banking 9%→23%); it carries a **510k-token context** at **$0.30/$1.20/$0.06 per Mtoken** [[upstage-solar-pro-4-benchmarks-and-analysis-9cbec43b]]. At 42 it sits ~15–21 points below the frontier cluster, so it is a cost-efficient mid-tier option, not a leader move. The roster sweep against artificialanalysis.ai surfaced no other benchmark or cost-field movements for any roster entity.

> Solar Pro 4 scores 42 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index ... a 27-point increase over Solar Pro 3. Its largest improvements on Solar Pro 3 are on agentic and long context work: Terminal-Bench v2.1 improves from 12% to 57%, AA-LCR from 31% to 71%, and τ³-Banking from 9% to 23%. [[upstage-solar-pro-4-benchmarks-and-analysis-9cbec43b]]

### Updates 2026-08-13

**SpaceXAI ships Grok 4.6 (Aug 12) — a post-training upgrade over Grok 4.5 that ties GPT-5.6 Sol for aggregate-index #3 at 60%+ lower per-token cost, and leads the frontier on token/turn efficiency.** SpaceXAI released **Grok 4.6** on 2026-08-12, holding the Grok 4.5 foundation constant and spending the delta on a longer supplemental training run, regenerated SFT trajectories, and agentic-environment RL — explicitly targeting long-running agents and more ambitious interactive/visual work [[introducing-grok-4-6-66321e57]]. It ships with a **500,000-token context**, at **unchanged $2/$6 per Mtoken** pricing, live day-one in **Cursor** and Grok Build plus the API, OpenRouter, Vercel and Cloudflare [[introducing-grok-4-6-66321e57]]. On the **AA Intelligence Index v4.1.1** it scores **61 (high)** — up **+5 over Grok 4.5 (54)** and +23 over Grok 4.3 in ~one month — **in line with GPT-5.6 Sol (max, 61)**, behind only Claude Opus 5 (max, 63) and Claude Fable 5 (62) and just ahead of Kimi K3 (57), so the aggregate #3 slot is now a **two-way tie between GPT-5.6 Sol and Grok 4.6** [[grok-4-6-returns-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-and-leads-on-cost-efficiency-bdc6284a]]. On **GDPval-AA v2** — the highest-weighted v4.1.1 evaluation — Grok 4.6 posts **Elo 1753, second only to Claude Opus 5** (1861), with confidence intervals overlapping Fable 5 and Qwen3.8 Max; it debuts on **AA-Briefcase at Elo 1577**, and posts **τ³-Banking 50.7% (top two)** and **Terminal-Bench v2.1 88.4% (level with the leaders)** [[grok-4-6-returns-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-and-leads-on-cost-efficiency-bdc6284a]]. The headline story is **cost efficiency, not raw intelligence**: AA measures Grok 4.6 resolving tasks in **~53 turns and ~0.5B input tokens** versus **~103 turns and ~2.0B input tokens for Claude Opus 5 (max)** — roughly half the turns and a quarter of the input tokens for comparable answers — which, layered on the 60%+ per-token discount ($2/$6 vs Opus 5 $5/$25 and Sol $5/$30), gives it a cost advantage well beyond its sticker price on long-horizon agentic loops [[grok-4-6-returns-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-and-leads-on-cost-efficiency-bdc6284a]]. This **supersedes Grok 4.5 (54)** as the xAI/SpaceXAI flagship and makes Grok 4.6 the leading cost-efficient pick for parallel-subagent fan-out and high-iteration game-dev tooling pipelines. Caveats: Grok 4.6's contamination-resistant coding placement (SWE-bench Pro) was not reported, and the Cursor-coupling concern deepens with a day-one Cursor launch. See [[grok-4.6]], [[grok-4.5]], [[gpt-5.6-sol]], [[claude-opus-5]], [[benchmark-leaders]], [[cost-comparison]].

### Updates 2026-08-12

**OpenAI ships GPT-5.6-Cyber — a GPT-5.6 Sol-derived, cyber-specialized model gated behind the new Daybreak Red tier; the headline "95.0% completion" is a willingness-to-respond metric, not a capability score.** On 2026-08-10 OpenAI expanded its Daybreak cybersecurity program and introduced **GPT-5.6-Cyber**, a variant **built on GPT-5.6 Sol** and fine-tuned to improve zero-day discovery and exploit-chain development while **reducing refusals on higher-risk dual-use cyber tasks** [[expanding-daybreak-as-the-cyber-defense-window-narrows-081601c2]]. It is available only through **Daybreak Red**, an applicant-vetted tier for advanced authorized workflows (proof-of-concept exploit development, exploit-chain validation, penetration testing, red teaming) requiring additional approval, verification, monitoring and human oversight; the lower **Daybreak Blue** tier serves defensive work on GPT-5.6 Sol [[expanding-daybreak-as-the-cyber-defense-window-narrows-081601c2]]. OpenAI reports GPT-5.6-Cyber **completes 95.0% of its internal Advanced Cybersecurity Completion Rate evaluation vs 1.5% for GPT-5.6 Sol** — but this metric measures how often the model *responds* to exploit-development / authn-bypass / privilege-escalation prompts (i.e. reduced refusals), not correctness, so it is **not comparable** to the aggregate-intelligence, contamination-resistant coding, or CyberGym axes this page tracks [[expanding-daybreak-as-the-cyber-defense-window-narrows-081601c2]]. As a real-world capability signal, OpenAI researchers used Daybreak Red to find **two previously unknown V8 (Chrome JS engine) vulnerabilities chainable to escape the heap sandbox**, patched by Google as CVE-2026-15903 [[expanding-daybreak-as-the-cyber-defense-window-narrows-081601c2]]. This is a **gated, specialized OpenAI variant**, not a change to the public aggregate-intelligence board (Claude Opus 5 max = 63 remains #1) or the AA Coding Agent Index (GPT-5.6 Sol = 80); it parallels Google's earlier Gemini 3.5 Flash Cyber and Anthropic's Fable 5 cyber-safeguard posture as labs field cyber-tuned frontier variants. No AA Intelligence Index / CyberGym placement was published, and the roster sweep against artificialanalysis.ai surfaced no benchmark or cost-field movements for any roster entity.

> Built on GPT-5.6 Sol, it is trained to improve capabilities on several specialized cybersecurity tasks (e.g., finding zero-day vulnerabilities and developing exploit chains) and to reduce refusals for certain higher-risk, dual-use cyber tasks. [[expanding-daybreak-as-the-cyber-defense-window-narrows-081601c2]]

> GPT-5.6-Cyber completes 95.0% of cybersecurity requests, compared with just 1.5% for GPT-5.6 Sol, and 2.0% when used with Daybreak Blue access. [[expanding-daybreak-as-the-cyber-defense-window-narrows-081601c2]]

### Updates 2026-08-11

Meta released **Muse Glimmer** on 2026-08-10, a 30B-parameter open-weights agentic model under Apache 2.0, distilled from Muse Spark 1.2 and optimized for local deployment [[muse-glimmer-benchmarks-and-analysis-e12a4eb2]]. It scores **35** on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index — far below frontier leaders (Claude Opus 5 at 63) but strong for its size class, and its differentiator is running the full context window on a single consumer GPU (single H100 at BF16, or an RTX 5090 / higher-spec MacBook at 4-bit) [[muse-glimmer-benchmarks-and-analysis-e12a4eb2]]. Trained for end-to-end agentic task completion, multi-step reasoning, and LLM-as-judge use, it is aimed at local coding agents rather than raw-intelligence competition — relevant to agentic-coding workflows that need an on-device tool-use model.

> Muse Glimmer (high) achieves a score of 35 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index.

> Muse Glimmer is a 30B-parameter model released under Apache 2.0. It's small enough to self-host on a single GPU, with weights at ~60 GB in BF16 and ~18 GB in 4-bit precision. The model can run at full context on a single H100 at BF16 precision, or on a higher-spec MacBook or RTX 5090 at 4-bit.

### Updates 2026-08-09

**Artificial Analysis ships Intelligence Index v4.1.1 (2026-08-06 patch) — top-three re-scaled up ~2 points, leader ordering unchanged; Claude Opus 5 (max) still #1, now at 63.** Artificial Analysis released a v4.1.1 patch of its Intelligence Index that upgrades grader models — HLE, AA-LCR and AA-Omniscience are now graded by GPT-5.6 Luna (medium) — and moves τ³-Banking to v1.0.1 [[launching-v4-1-1-of-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-8f7aaf85]]. AA frames it as a grading-robustness re-baseline, not a capability event: "a patch release ... Overall model rankings remain largely consistent, with a slight increase in scores due to improved grading robustness," with "most models moving by less than a point" [[launching-v4-1-1-of-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-8f7aaf85]]. The re-grade lifts the top cluster ~2 points without changing order: **Claude Opus 5 (max) 61 → 63 (still the aggregate #1)**, **Claude Fable 5 (max, Opus 4.8 fallback) 60 → 62 (#2)**, **GPT-5.6 Sol (max) 59 → 61 (#3)**, while **Kimi K3 holds at 57 (#4 overall and the confirmed open-weights leader)** [[launching-v4-1-1-of-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-8f7aaf85]]. Because the shift comes from grader/task-version changes rather than capability, cross-version numeric comparisons (v4.1 → v4.1.1) are again not strictly like-for-like — the ~2-point top-cluster lift is a methodology artifact, not a capability move. v4.1.1 now incorporates 9 evaluations (GDPval-AA v2, τ³-Banking, Terminal-Bench v2.1, SciCode, HLE, GPQA Diamond, CritPt, AA-Omniscience, AA-LCR) [[launching-v4-1-1-of-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-8f7aaf85]].

### Updates 2026-08-04

Alibaba shipped its new flagship **Qwen3.8 Max**, which Artificial Analysis placed at **53 on the AA Intelligence Index (v4.1)** — a new Alibaba per-lab flagship (superseding the Qwen3.7 Max line) that slots between **Grok 4.5 (54)** and ties **Claude Sonnet 5 (53)**, sitting below the unchanged top cluster of **Opus 5 (61) / Fable 5 (60) / GPT-5.6 Sol (59) / Kimi K3 (57)** — so it does not move the frontier leader [[qwen3-8-max-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-0ad85146]]. It is a **2.4T-total / ~95B-active Mixture-of-Experts** model with a **1M-token context** and **text/image/video input**, priced at **$2/$6 per Mtoken** (blended ~$1.18/Mtok) — cheaper than Kimi K3's $3/$15 and roughly matching Grok 4.5's $2/$6 [[qwen3-8-max-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-0ad85146]]. The headline caveats are latency and verbosity: it emits only **46.5 tok/sec** (well below the ~72 tok/sec median for its price tier) and AA flags it as "notably slow and very verbose," a real cost/latency multiplier on output-dominated agentic loops [[qwen3-8-max-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-0ad85146]]. Weights are **not currently public** (proprietary at launch, served via Alibaba Cloud); an open-weights release plus a smaller 27B variant have been floated in launch coverage but remain unconfirmed (see Open questions).

> Qwen3.8 Max scores 53 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index, placing it well above average among comparable models (median: 32). [[qwen3-8-max-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-0ad85146]]

> Qwen3.8 Max is amongst the leading models in intelligence and reasonably priced when comparing to other models of similar price, though it's also notably slow and very verbose. [[qwen3-8-max-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-0ad85146]]

### Updates 2026-08-03

**MiniMax H3 open weights land on Hugging Face (`MiniMaxAI/MiniMax-H3`) — resolving the 2026-08-02 open question — as two task-specific omni-modal checkpoints totaling 288 GB under a restrictive MiniMax Community License.** The open-weights drop MiniMax promised "in the coming days" at H3's July 31 API launch [[minimax-releases-minimax-h3-an-omni-modal-video-model-that-generates-15-second-2k-clips-with-native-stereo-audio-53db5121]] shipped to the `MiniMaxAI` Hugging Face org, confirming the previously-unverified secondary reporting against a first-party artifact [[minimaxai-minimax-h3-hugging-face-1f0e60fd]]. The release is structured as **two task-specific checkpoints**, each a self-contained repo bundling a specialized Omni Transformer Model plus processor, tokenizer, text encoder, Visual VAE and a standalone Audio VAE, for a **total repository size of 288 GB** [[minimaxai-minimax-h3-hugging-face-1f0e60fd]]. Licensing is the key caveat: the **MiniMax H3 Community License Agreement** is non-commercial-free and grants commercial use only to organizations under $20M annual revenue (with attribution), and MiniMax notes open weights are **temporarily region-limited** pending a clearer regulatory/compliance framework — materially less permissive than Apache-2.0, Modified-MIT, or MIT open-weights releases [[minimaxai-minimax-h3-hugging-face-1f0e60fd]]. On Artificial Analysis's video leaderboards H3 ranks #1 in Video Editing, #2 in Text-to-Video and #3 in Image-to-Video [[minimaxai-minimax-h3-hugging-face-1f0e60fd]]. This does **not** move the frontier-LLM current-state picture: H3 is an omni-modal video model, not an aggregate-intelligence LLM, so the LLM leader board is unchanged.

> MiniMax H3 is released as two task-specific checkpoints, each containing a specialized Omni Transformer Model together with the required processor, tokenizer, text encoder, Visual VAE, and standalone Audio VAE components. The total repository size is 288 GB. The weights are released under the MiniMax H3 Community License Agreement, which permits commercial use for organizations under $20M annual revenue with attribution, though open weights are temporarily limited in certain regions until the regulatory and compliance framework becomes clearer. [[minimaxai-minimax-h3-hugging-face-1f0e60fd]]

### Updates 2026-08-01

**DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 goes official: Terminal-Bench jumps from 61.8 to 82.7, DeepSWE from 7.3 to 54.4, AA Intelligence Index from 40 to 50 — via re-post-training, not a new architecture.** DeepSeek published the official release of V4-Flash-0731 on Hugging Face on July 31, 2026 and moved the API into public beta, explicitly superseding the preview that had been in limited access [[deepseek-ai-deepseek-v4-flash-0731-a1a747f4]] [[deepseek-upgrades-deepseek-v4-flash-0731-with-major-agentic-and-coding-gains-159beea4]]. The architecture and total parameter count are unchanged from the preview; all gains derive from re-post-training — specifically, greatly enhanced agentic and long-horizon coding capabilities. The model scores **82.7 on Terminal Bench 2.1** (vs 61.8 for the Flash Preview), **54.4 on DeepSWE** (vs 7.3 for the preview), and **50 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index** (up 10 points from the preview's 40, and now matching the highest-scoring open-weights small models) [[deepseek-v4-flash-0731-scores-50-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-10-points-above-previous-deepseek-v4-flash-108621d0]] [[deepseek-upgrades-deepseek-v4-flash-0731-with-major-agentic-and-coding-gains-159beea4]]. Despite its smaller activated parameter count it outperforms DeepSeek-V4-Pro (Preview) on the published benchmark suite [[deepseek-ai-deepseek-v4-flash-0731-a1a747f4]]. The model is **MIT-licensed** and **ungated**; pricing is **$0.14/MTok input on cache miss, $0.0028/MTok cache hit, $0.28/MTok output** — making the cache-hit rate extremely attractive for agentic loops that reuse a shared system prompt; it also ships with the **DSpark speculative decoding module** [[deepseek-upgrades-deepseek-v4-flash-0731-with-major-agentic-and-coding-gains-159beea4]]. For the aggregate roster this does **not** displace any leader: V4-Flash-0731 at 50 still sits below Kimi K3 (57), Opus 4.8 (56), GPT-5.5 (55), Sonnet 5 (53) and above GLM-5.2 (51)/Muse Spark 1.1 (51) in the open-weights sub-ranking; its significance is the **agentic performance surprise** — scoring above Opus 4.8 and matching GPT-5.5 on Terminal-Bench 2.1 despite its far smaller activated parameter count. Counter-argument: the 10-point AA Index jump (40→50) looks dramatic but the re-post-training gains are concentrated in agentic/coding tasks; the prior open-weights intelligence leader Kimi K3 (57) still leads by 7 points on the aggregate index, and DeepSWE 54.4 is substantially below the 67.4% Kimi K3 scored on their V4-Flash comparison set.

> DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731 scores 50 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index, 10 points above the previous DeepSeek V4 Flash. DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 outperforms DeepSeek-V4-Pro (Preview) on the published benchmarks despite its far smaller activated parameter count. [[deepseek-v4-flash-0731-scores-50-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-10-points-above-previous-deepseek-v4-flash-108621d0]]

> DeepSeek published DeepSeek-V4-Flash-0731 on Hugging Face and moved the official V4-Flash API into public beta on July 31, 2026. The model card is explicit that this is the official release superseding the preview, and that the architecture and size are unchanged. The gains come from re-post-training, not a new design. It scores 82.7 on Terminal Bench 2.1 (compared to 61.8 for the Flash Preview) and 54.4 on DeepSWE, up from 7.3 for the preview. MIT-licensed and ungated. DeepSeek's pricing page lists deepseek-v4-flash at $0.14 per 1M input tokens on a cache miss, $0.0028 on a cache hit, and $0.28 per 1M output tokens. The checkpoint ships with the DSpark speculative decoding module attached. [[deepseek-upgrades-deepseek-v4-flash-0731-with-major-agentic-and-coding-gains-159beea4]]

**MiniMax H3 launches: 15-second 2K video with native stereo audio from a single unified omni-modal model at < 1/3 the cost of mainstream alternatives.** MiniMax released MiniMax H3 on August 1, 2026 via API (open weights "in the coming days") [[minimax-releases-minimax-h3-an-omni-modal-video-model-that-generates-15-second-2k-clips-with-native-stereo-audio-53db5121]]. H3's defining architectural claim is **unified omni-modal pretraining**: text, image, video, and audio context are joint inputs, so reference, editing, and motion relationships are expressed in natural language rather than through separate conditioning heads. This collapses the six previously-distinct video tasks (text-to-video, image-to-video, first-and-last-frame, subject reference, motion reference, video editing) into one model [[minimax-releases-minimax-h3-an-omni-modal-video-model-that-generates-15-second-2k-clips-with-native-stereo-audio-53db5121]]. At **2K**, H3's per-second price is less than a third of mainstream models; at 768p, less than half the price of mainstream 720p — both figures without a published comparison baseline, so independent cost validation is pending. Relevance to game-dev: a unified video model that handles motion reference and editing in natural language could significantly reduce the bespoke conditioning pipeline work required for game cinematics and concept-art animatics; at sub-1/3 cost it also changes the calculus on iterative concept video generation at production scale. Counter-argument: the open weights release is announced but not yet shipped ("coming days"); cost claims lack a named comparison baseline; 15-second at 2K is a hardware-intensive serving target with no published minimum GPU spec.

> MiniMax H3 generates 15-second 2K video with native stereo audio from unified text, image, video, and audio context. Previous video stacks split into text-to-video, image-to-video, first-and-last-frame, subject reference, motion reference, and video editing; MiniMax H3 folds those into one pretraining paradigm where reference and editing relationships are expressed in natural language. At 2K, H3's per-second price is less than a third of mainstream models; at 768p, less than half the price of mainstream 720p. Open weights are promised 'in the coming days,' not shipped; the API is the only path today. [[minimax-releases-minimax-h3-an-omni-modal-video-model-that-generates-15-second-2k-clips-with-native-stereo-audio-53db5121]]

### Updates 2026-07-31

**Thinking Machines ships Inkling-Small — a 276B-total / 12B-active Apache-2.0 open-weights MoE that lands at AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 40, within a point of the 975B Inkling (41) at less than a third of the parameters.** Released July 31, 2026, Inkling-Small is a multimodal Mixture-of-Experts reasoning model with controllable reasoning effort that Thinking Machines says "matches Inkling at a quarter of the size," with native reasoning over audio and images and a context window of up to 1M tokens (256K as served/measured by Artificial Analysis) [[introducing-inkling-small-5c51c45f]]. Artificial Analysis independently places it at **40 on the Intelligence Index v4.1** — one point behind Inkling (41) — and reports that **no open-weights model at its size or smaller scores higher on the Intelligence Index** [[inkling-small-lands-within-a-point-of-inkling-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-with-less-than-a-third-of-the-parameters-805cabb6]]. On task coverage it ties Inkling on Terminal-Bench 2.1 (55%) and edges it on Humanity's Last Exam (32% vs 30%), GPQA Diamond (89% vs 87%), CritPt (8% vs 5%) and SciCode (49% vs 46%), while being more efficient per task [[inkling-small-lands-within-a-point-of-inkling-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-with-less-than-a-third-of-the-parameters-805cabb6]]. For a cost-sensitive agentic-coding / game-dev tooling loop this is a directly relevant compact, self-hostable, Apache-2.0-licensed option — a more permissive license than Kimi K3 (Modified MIT) or Laguna S 2.1 (OpenMDW-1.1) and small enough (12B active) to run far more cheaply than the ~1.4TB aggregate open-weights leader Kimi K3. It does **not** move aggregate leadership: Claude Opus 5 (v4.1 = 61) remains #1, Kimi K3 (57) remains the open-weights aggregate leader, and Inkling (41) remains the US-open-weights aggregate leader; Inkling-Small is a compact best-in-size-class entry. This resolves the prior open question on the [[inkling]] entity that Inkling-Small lacked an independent AA placement.

> Inkling Small scores 40 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index, within a point of its flagship sibling Inkling with less than a third of the total and active parameters. No open weights model at its size or smaller scores higher on the Intelligence Index. Inkling Small is an open weights reasoning model with 276B total parameters (12B active MoE), text, image, and speech input, and a 256K token context window. [[inkling-small-lands-within-a-point-of-inkling-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-with-less-than-a-third-of-the-parameters-805cabb6]]

> Inkling-Small is a multimodal, Mixture-of-Experts model with controllable reasoning effort that matches Inkling at a quarter of the size. Like Inkling, it features native reasoning over audio and images, variable thinking effort, a context window of up to 1M tokens, and well-rounded performance across a range of benchmarks. [[introducing-inkling-small-5c51c45f]]

_no contradictions or gaps known yet (last reviewed: 2026-07-30)_

### Updates 2026-07-29

**Poolside ships Laguna S 2.1 — a compact 118B/8B-active open-weight agentic-coding MoE that beats models ~10x its size on Terminal-Bench 2.1.** Released July 21, 2026, Laguna S 2.1 is a 118-billion-parameter Mixture-of-Experts model activating only 8B parameters per token, built specifically for agentic coding and long-horizon work with a 1M-token context window [[introducing-laguna-s-2-1-131cc339]]. Poolside reports it scores **70.2% on Terminal-Bench 2.1** (its agent harness, thinking enabled) — placing it 11th on the company's compiled leaderboard and **ahead of DeepSeek-V4-Pro-Max (a 1.6-trillion-parameter model that scored 64.0) despite being roughly 10x smaller in total parameters** — with **59.4% on SWE-bench Pro's public dataset** and 78.5% on SWE-bench Multilingual [[poolside-drops-laguna-s-2-1-an-open-weight-coding-model-that-beats-rivals-10x-its-size-37368a6e]]. The weights ship on Hugging Face under the permissive **OpenMDW-1.1** license, and the model's compact active size makes it **runnable on a single NVIDIA DGX Spark** (or locally via vLLM, SGLang, Ollama, llama.cpp, MLX, TensorRT-LLM) [[introducing-laguna-s-2-1-131cc339]]. This is directly relevant to a cost-sensitive agentic-coding / game-dev tooling loop: unlike the current aggregate open-weights leader Kimi K3 (~1.4TB, multi-node H100/B200 only), Laguna S 2.1 is small enough to self-host on a single high-end box while still claiming frontier-tier agentic-coding scores. **Caveat:** Laguna S 2.1 has **no independent Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index aggregate placement** — every headline number is on Poolside's own harness / self-compiled leaderboard — so it does not displace Kimi K3 (57) as the aggregate open-weights leader; it is a compact-efficiency / locally-runnable best-in-segment entry. The "West's most capable open-weight model" claim is filed under `## Disputes`.

> Laguna S 2.1 is the most capable agentic coding model in its weight class by a wide margin. S 2.1 scores 70.2% on Terminal-Bench 2.1 in Poolside's agent harness with thinking enabled. Laguna S 2.1 is a 118B total parameter Mixture-of-Experts model with 8B activated parameters per token, built for agentic coding and long-horizon work. [[introducing-laguna-s-2-1-131cc339]]

> Laguna S 2.1 scores 70.2% on Terminal-Bench 2.1, placing it 11th on the company's compiled leaderboard — ahead of DeepSeek-V4-Pro-Max, a 1.6-trillion-parameter model that scored 64.0. On SWE-Bench Multilingual, it posts 78.5%, and on SWE-Bench Pro's public dataset, 59.4%. The weights are available on Hugging Face under an OpenMDW-1.1 license. It is small enough to run on a single NVIDIA DGX Spark. [[poolside-drops-laguna-s-2-1-an-open-weight-coding-model-that-beats-rivals-10x-its-size-37368a6e]]

### Updates 2026-07-28

**Kimi K3's open weights top LMArena's Frontend Code Arena at 1679 — a 17-place jump ahead of Opus 4.8 and GPT-5.6 Sol on frontend coding — and post the fastest release growth on Hugging Face.** Within hours of the July 27 weights drop, Kimi K3 climbed from #18 to #1 on the Frontend Code Arena leaderboard at 1679, ahead of Anthropic's Opus 4.8 and OpenAI's GPT-5.6 Sol on blind frontend-coding preference [[moonshot-ai-releases-kimi-k3-s-weights-sees-fastest-release-growth-ever-on-hugging-face-4bcc3667]]. This is the first blind-arena axis on which the leading open-weights model beats the closed flagships, and it lands squarely in the game-dev / UI lens. It does not overturn aggregate leadership (Opus 5 = 61 on the AA Intelligence Index v4.1) or contamination-resistant coding (Fable 5 still leads SWE-bench Pro at 80.0%) — a benchmark-methodology divergence filed under `## Disputes`.

> Within hours of release, Kimi K3 jumped from #18 to #1 on the Frontend Code Arena leaderboard with a score of 1679, ahead of Anthropic's Opus 4.8 and OpenAI's GPT-5.6 Sol on frontend coding tasks. [[moonshot-ai-releases-kimi-k3-s-weights-sees-fastest-release-growth-ever-on-hugging-face-4bcc3667]]

> Moonshot AI released the weights of Kimi K3 and saw the fastest release growth ever on Hugging Face. [[moonshot-ai-releases-kimi-k3-s-weights-sees-fastest-release-growth-ever-on-hugging-face-4bcc3667]]

**Alibaba's Qwen3.8-Max preview firms up the queued 2.4T open-weights challenger — but ships without a single published benchmark.** Previewed at WAIC Shanghai on July 19 (three days after Kimi K3), Qwen3.8-Max is a 2.4-trillion-parameter multimodal MoE that Alibaba's Qwen team describes as "second only to Fable 5" among the systems it benchmarked [[alibaba-previews-qwen3-8-max-a-2-4-trillion-parameter-multimodal-model-days-after-moonshot-s-kimi-k3-open-weight-launch-7cb772fb]]. This provides a primary source for what the wiki previously carried only second-hand (Nathan Lambert's report of a queued 2.4T Qwen 3.8). It is preview-only: no evaluation tables, no model card, no license, and no date for the promised open weights, so the "second only to Fable 5" claim is unverified — filed as an open question.

> Alibaba previewed Qwen3.8-Max, a 2.4 trillion-parameter multimodal Mixture-of-Experts model, describing it as second only to Fable 5 among the systems it benchmarked. [[alibaba-previews-qwen3-8-max-a-2-4-trillion-parameter-multimodal-model-days-after-moonshot-s-kimi-k3-open-weight-launch-7cb772fb]]
