### Updates 2026-07-27

**Kimi K3 open weights confirmed landed on Hugging Face — MXFP4 QAT release, 1.4TB, Blackwell/MI400 native, leads SWE Marathon and Program Bench.** The full 2.8T Kimi K3 weights dropped on Hugging Face on July 27, 2026 as scheduled, under a Modified MIT license, making it the confirmed leading open-weights model (AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 57) by a wide margin over GLM-5.2 (51) [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]]. The release format is **MXFP4 (Microscaling FP4)**, applied via **quantization-aware training starting from the SFT stage** rather than post-training quantization — a first-party claim that model quality degradation is substantially lower than standard PTQ [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]]. MXFP4 is natively supported by NVIDIA Blackwell GPUs and AMD MI400, and the full-model weight footprint is approximately **1.4TB** (~594GB BF16), bringing self-hosting within reach of multi-node H100/B200 clusters [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]]. On task coverage, **K3 leads all models on SWE Marathon and Program Bench**, consistent with its 1M-context advantage on sustained coding sessions; Moonshot's Mooncake disaggregated inference infrastructure (prefill/decode split across node pools, 90% cache hit rate on coding workloads) enables an aggressive API **cached input price of $0.30/MTok** [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]]. The prior dispute about whether K3 was truly open-weights is now resolved-toward-open; see `## Disputes` below.

> Moonshot AI publicly released Kimi K3 on July 16, 2026, with full open-source weights promised by July 27. At 2.8 trillion parameters, it is the first open-source model to reach the 3-trillion-parameter class. [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]]

> MXFP4 weights (Microscaling FP4): Each weight is stored in 4-bit floating point with per-block scaling factors. MXFP4 is supported natively by NVIDIA Blackwell GPUs and AMD MI400 accelerators, making deployment hardware-friendly. [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]]

> K3 leads all models on SWE Marathon and Program Bench, suggesting particular strength in sustained coding sessions — consistent with the 1M-token context window enabling full-repository understanding. [[kimi-k3-model-overview-2-8t-parameters-mxfp4-quantization-and-what-the-open-weights-mean-for-the-community-67bfe96b]]

### Updates 2026-07-26

Artificial Analysis published its primary evaluation of **Claude Opus 5**, resolving the placement that was pending since the July 24 launch: **Opus 5 (max) scores 61 on the AA Intelligence Index v4.1 and is narrowly the most intelligent model in the world**, edging Claude Fable 5 (max, 60) and ahead of GPT-5.6 Sol (max, 59) and Kimi K3 (57) [[opus-5-fable-5-level-intelligence-at-a-lower-cost-per-task-864f9aa5]]. This supersedes the prior wiki framing that the public aggregate-index board still led with Fable 5 pending Opus 5 confirmation.

> Claude Opus 5 (max) scores 61 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index, effectively tied with Claude Fable 5 (max, 60), and ahead of GPT-5.6 Sol (max, 59), Kimi K3 (57). [[opus-5-fable-5-level-intelligence-at-a-lower-cost-per-task-864f9aa5]]

Crucially, Opus 5 reaches Fable-5-class intelligence **at lower cost**: AA reports it is **~26% cheaper per Intelligence Index task ($2.03 vs Fable 5's $2.75)** despite Fable 5 listing at 2× the per-token price ($10/$50 vs Opus 5's $5/$25) [[opus-5-fable-5-level-intelligence-at-a-lower-cost-per-task-864f9aa5]]. For an agentic-coding or game-dev loop this makes Opus 5 the value-default flagship on both quality and cost-per-task.

> Claude Opus 5 is narrowly the most intelligent model on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index, offering comparable intelligence to Fable 5 at 26% lower Cost per Task. Claude Opus 5 (max) costs $2.03 on average per Intelligence Index task, below Claude Fable 5 (with fallback) at $2.75. [[opus-5-fable-5-level-intelligence-at-a-lower-cost-per-task-864f9aa5]]

On the agentic-knowledge-work axes Opus 5 also takes the lead: it posts the **highest GDPval-AA v2 score recorded at 1861 Elo (>100 points ahead of Fable 5 and GPT-5.6 Sol max)** and leads **AA-Briefcase at 1720 Elo (+146 over Fable 5)** [[claude-opus-5-the-new-leader-in-agentic-knowledge-work-92cf92da]]. Fable 5 retains its SWE-bench Pro (80.0%) edge, so absolute-quality long-horizon coding leadership is unchanged; what moved today is aggregate-index, GDPval-AA v2, AA-Briefcase, and cost-per-task leadership, all now held by Opus 5.

> On GDPval-AA v2, Claude Opus 5 (max) scores 1861 Elo, more than 100 points ahead of Claude Fable 5 and GPT-5.6 Sol (max), making it a leader in agentic knowledge work tasks. On AA-Briefcase, Claude Opus 5 (max) scores 1720 Elo, +146 ahead of Fable 5. [[claude-opus-5-the-new-leader-in-agentic-knowledge-work-92cf92da]]

### Updates 2026-07-25

Anthropic shipped **Claude Opus 5** on July 24, 2026 — its **fourth model in under two months** (after Fable 5 in early June and Sonnet 5 at end of June) — as the new **standard flagship** replacing Opus 4.8, positioned to come "close to the frontier intelligence of the larger Claude Fable 5 while running at Opus speed and Opus pricing" [[introducing-claude-opus-5-c34a3276]]. It lists at **$5/$25 per Mtoken — unchanged from Opus 4.8 and about half of Fable 5's $10/$50** [[anthropic-launches-claude-opus-5-its-fourth-model-in-two-months-and-it-tops-fable-5-on-most-benchmarks-7253a489]], with a research-preview **Fast mode ~2.5x faster at $10/$50** and a **1M-token context window** [[introducing-claude-opus-5-c34a3276]] [[meet-the-new-claude-opus-5-frontier-class-agentic-coding-and-computer-use-at-unchanged-opus-pricing-7f7892b3]].

Opus 5 **tops Fable 5 on most benchmarks at half the token price** [[anthropic-launches-claude-opus-5-its-fourth-model-in-two-months-and-it-tops-fable-5-on-most-benchmarks-7253a489]]: leads **Frontier-Bench** at **43.3% max effort** vs Fable 5's 33.7% and GPT-5.6 Sol's 37.5%; posts a verified **30.16% on ARC-AGI-3** (~3x the next model); **70.57% on OSWorld 2.0** (vs Opus 4.8's 55.7%); and **26.0% on Zapier AutomationBench** (vs Opus 4.8 17.0%, Fable 5 17.4%) [[meet-the-new-claude-opus-5-frontier-class-agentic-coding-and-computer-use-at-unchanged-opus-pricing-7f7892b3]]. **Fable 5 still edges Opus 5 on SWE-bench Pro (80.0%) and remains Anthropic's recommendation for the most advanced multi-day autonomous agent work** [[meet-the-new-claude-opus-5-frontier-class-agentic-coding-and-computer-use-at-unchanged-opus-pricing-7f7892b3]] [[introducing-claude-opus-5-c34a3276]]. Opus 5 also introduces an **effort dial (low→high plus a max tier) with thinking on by default** [[introducing-claude-opus-5-c34a3276]].

> Claude Opus 5 is our most intelligent model, delivering frontier performance for agentic coding, computer use, and complex reasoning at the same price as Claude Opus 4.8: $5 per million input tokens and $25 per million output tokens. [[introducing-claude-opus-5-c34a3276]]

> Opus 5 reached 70.57% on OSWorld 2.0 against 55.7% for Opus 4.8. On Zapier AutomationBench it scored 26.0%, against 17.0% for Opus 4.8 and 17.4% for Fable 5. Fable 5 still edges Opus 5 on SWE-bench Pro at 80.0%. [[meet-the-new-claude-opus-5-frontier-class-agentic-coding-and-computer-use-at-unchanged-opus-pricing-7f7892b3]]

> Opus 5 is the fourth model Anthropic has shipped in under two months, following Fable 5 in early June, Sonnet 5 at the end of June, and now Opus 5 on July 24. It costs $5/$25 per million tokens, the same price as Opus 4.8, and about half the price of Fable 5. [[anthropic-launches-claude-opus-5-its-fourth-model-in-two-months-and-it-tops-fable-5-on-most-benchmarks-7253a489]]

### Updates 2026-07-24

**Kimi K3 is an open-weights model, not a "proprietary closed flagship": full 2.8T weights scheduled for Hugging Face on July 27, 2026 under a Modified MIT license — which would make it the leading open-weights model (AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 57) by a wide margin.** Moonshot AI's first-party tech blog describes Kimi K3 as "the world's first open 3T-class model," and the weights drop (widely reported for July 27, 2026, under the same Modified MIT license as K2.6/K2.7-Code) would place it well above the current open-weights leader GLM-5.2 (v4.1 = 51) and DeepSeek V4-Pro (44) [[kimi-k3-tech-blog-open-frontier-intelligence-0d83e2aa]] [[kimi-k3-s-open-weights-arrive-july-27-the-catch-is-1-4tb-56e05201]] [[kimi-k3-achieves-3-in-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-comparable-to-opus-4-8-and-gpt-5-5-bce8423e]]. This supersedes the topic Summary's per-lab framing of K3 as "a proprietary closed flagship ... despite weights not being released." Nathan Lambert (Interconnects) calls K3 "the strongest open model ever released" and "a watershed moment because frontier open-weight models are now real," and reports Alibaba has queued a 2.4-trillion-parameter Qwen 3.8 open-weights model "coming soon" — signaling an escalating open-weights race at the 2–3T scale [[kimi-k3-the-open-weights-escalation-be621fe3]].

The first-party blog also resolves the standing dense-vs-MoE question: K3 is a **Mixture-of-Experts** model activating **16 of 896 experts (~50B active params)** per token via a Stable LatentMoE framework, built on **Kimi Delta Attention** (a gated delta-rule linear-attention variant using channel-wise rather than scalar forgetting) and **Attention Residuals**, together delivering ~2.5x better scaling efficiency than Kimi K2 [[kimi-k3-tech-blog-open-frontier-intelligence-0d83e2aa]]. The open-weights advantage carries a real self-hosting caveat: the weights total ~1.4TB (≈594GB in BF16; community GGUF Q4 ~300–400GB) [[kimi-k3-s-open-weights-arrive-july-27-the-catch-is-1-4tb-56e05201]].

> Kimi K3 is the world's first open 3T-class model — frontier performance across coding, knowledge work, and reasoning, with native multimodality and 1M context. [[kimi-k3-tech-blog-open-frontier-intelligence-0d83e2aa]]

> Kimi K3 has scaled up Mixture of Experts (MoE) sparsity, effectively activating 16 out of 896 experts when paired with a Stable LatentMoE framework ... these structural changes yield an approximate 2.5x improvement in overall scaling efficiency compared to Kimi K2. [[kimi-k3-tech-blog-open-frontier-intelligence-0d83e2aa]]

> Kimi K3's open weights arrive July 27. The catch is 1.4TB ... The model is reported at approximately 594 GB in BF16 format, with community GGUF quantizations (Q4) likely reducing this to 300–400 GB. [[kimi-k3-s-open-weights-arrive-july-27-the-catch-is-1-4tb-56e05201]]

> [Kimi K3 is] the strongest open model ever released ... a watershed moment because frontier open-weight models are now real. [[kimi-k3-the-open-weights-escalation-be621fe3]]

### Updates 2026-07-23

**BusinessCaseBench: cross-model oracle reaches 92.8% on business knowledge work, with advisory tasks remaining the hardest tier for all frontier models.** A new arXiv benchmark [[frontier-ai-performance-across-the-business-disciplines-a-case-grounded-benchmark-of-knowledge-work-and-analytical-reasoning-72a97182]] evaluates open-ended analytical knowledge work using 238 Harvard-style business cases paired with instructor solutions, yielding 615 questions across 18 disciplines (Strategy, Finance, Marketing, Operations, Leadership & Organizational Behavior, and more). GPT-5.4, Claude Sonnet 4.6, and Gemini 3 Flash Preview were evaluated on all 615 questions. The key cross-model result: a **cross-model oracle** (best score from any of the three) achieves **92.8%**, 4.5 points above the leading single model, confirming that frontier-model knowledge is distributed rather than absent — the gap is about completeness and consistent thoroughness, not fundamental missing knowledge. Fewer than 7% of questions defeat all three models simultaneously. The hardest task categories are open-ended advisory and opportunity-identification (e.g., identifying organizational opportunities, advising on financial matters), while structured analytical and explanatory tasks approach ceiling. This is consistent with AA-Briefcase's finding that presentation and rubric completeness — not analytical depth — distinguish the top frontier models on knowledge-work evaluations. The 7% "all-fail" tail represents the genuine remaining challenge for human-AI collaboration on knowledge work.

> BusinessCaseBench evaluates open-ended analytical knowledge work using 238 business school cases paired with instructor solutions, yielding 615 questions across eighteen disciplines from Strategy and Finance to Leadership & Organizational Behavior.

> A cross-model oracle that takes the best score among the three frontier models reaches 92.8%, which is 4.5 percentage points above the leading single model.

> Fewer than 7% of questions defeat every frontier model, indicating that the required knowledge is distributed across the frontier rather than absent from it, and that complete and thorough responses to highly open-ended analytical tasks remain the main challenge and opportunity for human-AI collaboration.

**Roster re-verification — leader picture unchanged (2026-07-23).** No new first-party model release, benchmark re-placement, or pricing change from Anthropic, OpenAI, Google, xAI, Meta, Moonshot AI, Z.ai, or MiniMax on 2026-07-23. The publicly-accessible aggregate-intelligence picture from 2026-07-22 holds: Claude Fable 5 (AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 60) leads, GPT-5.6 Sol (max) = 59 is #2, Kimi K3 = 57, Opus 4.8 = 56, GPT-5.5 = 55, Grok 4.5 = 54, Sonnet 5 = 53, Gemini 3.6 Flash (Flash-tier) = 50, GLM-5.2 (open-weights #1) = 51, and Gemini 3.1 Pro = 46.

### Updates 2026-07-22

**Google ships Gemini 3.6 Flash (July 22, 2026) — same AA Intelligence Index score (50) as Gemini 3.5 Flash but half the time per task, 17% fewer output tokens, output price cut from $9 to $7.50 per Mtoken, and 275 tok/sec throughput.** Google released Gemini 3.6 Flash as the new Flash-tier workhorse alongside Gemini 3.5 Flash-Lite and Gemini 3.5 Flash Cyber [[introducing-gemini-3-6-flash-3-5-flash-lite-and-3-5-flash-cyber-268e6f2a]]. Artificial Analysis confirms Gemini 3.6 Flash scores **50 on the AA Intelligence Index** — identical to Gemini 3.5 Flash — but the efficiency story is more significant: it halves time per task relative to 3.5 Flash, generates 17% fewer output tokens, and reaches 275 tokens/second throughput, translating to a cost-per-task drop of approximately 18% ($0.59 → $0.50) [[gemini-3-6-flash-and-gemini-3-5-flash-lite-halving-time-per-task-e581ad77]]. Pricing is $1.50 input / $7.50 output per Mtoken — the same $1.50 input as 3.5 Flash but output reduced from $9 [[introducing-gemini-3-6-flash-3-5-flash-lite-and-3-5-flash-cyber-268e6f2a]]. Alongside 3.6 Flash, Google shipped **Gemini 3.5 Flash-Lite** (AA Intelligence Index = 36, most cost-efficient Google option) and **Gemini 3.5 Flash Cyber** (cybersecurity-specialized variant). **This replaces Gemini 3.5 Flash as Google's Flash-tier reference model** — the per-lab Google section should now cite 3.6 Flash as the Flash workhorse; the aggregate-intelligence leader picture is unchanged (Fable 5 = 60 still leads, 3.6 Flash at 50 does not displace Gemini 3.1 Pro at 46 for the Pro tier). The "Gemini 3.5 Flash (v4.1 = 50)" framing in the July 21 non-events note is now superseded.

> Gemini 3.6 Flash is our latest workhorse model, delivering better coding, knowledge work, and multimodal performance at a lower price than 3.5 Flash. [[introducing-gemini-3-6-flash-3-5-flash-lite-and-3-5-flash-cyber-268e6f2a]]

> Gemini 3.6 Flash scores 50 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index, maintaining the same intelligence as Gemini 3.5 Flash while halving time per task relative to its predecessor and increasing token efficiency. [[gemini-3-6-flash-and-gemini-3-5-flash-lite-halving-time-per-task-e581ad77]]

> At 275 tokens per second, Gemini 3.6 Flash is notably fast. Gemini 3.5 Flash-Lite scores 36 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index. [[gemini-3-6-flash-and-gemini-3-5-flash-lite-halving-time-per-task-e581ad77]]

**Kimi K3 places #2 on AA-Briefcase (Elo 1543) behind Fable 5 (1574), and leads Fable 5 on analytical quality (1754 vs 1744) — but costs $10.57/task and takes 56.4 minutes on average.** Artificial Analysis published an AA-Briefcase profile of Kimi K3, adding depth to the July 17–19 aggregate-index analysis [[kimi-k3-second-only-to-fable-5-on-aa-briefcase-a126a86d]]. On AA-Briefcase — the frontier knowledge-work evaluation first announced alongside the AA Intelligence Index v4.1 methodology — Kimi K3 scores an Elo of **1543 (+727 over Kimi K2.6)**, second only to Claude Fable 5 (1574) and with a **rubric pass rate of 51%** (Fable 5: 56%). The analytical quality sub-axis shows Kimi K3's strongest showing: **1754 analytical quality Elo, slightly above Fable 5's 1744** — a result that partially counterweights the lower aggregate AA Intelligence Index score (57 vs 60). Presentation quality is weaker (1471 Elo). The cost picture is the most significant caveat for agentic use: **$10.57 per AA-Briefcase task, averaging 83 turns (vs 67 for Fable 5), at 56.4 minutes per task** — placing it among the most expensive models to run on this workload despite the sub-$Opus pricing at the per-Mtoken level [[kimi-k3-second-only-to-fable-5-on-aa-briefcase-a126a86d]].

> On AA-Briefcase, Kimi K3 scores an Elo of 1543, a +727 improvement over Kimi K2.6 and the second highest score recorded, behind only Claude Fable 5 (1574). [[kimi-k3-second-only-to-fable-5-on-aa-briefcase-a126a86d]]

> Kimi K3 achieves a rubric pass rate of 51%, second only to Claude Fable 5 (56%) and ahead of Claude Sonnet 5 (max, 42.3%) and GPT-5.6 Sol (max, 41.8%). It records an analytical quality Elo of 1754, comparable to Claude Fable 5 (1744). [[kimi-k3-second-only-to-fable-5-on-aa-briefcase-a126a86d]]

### Updates 2026-07-21

No new first-party frontier-model release, benchmark re-placement, or price change met this topic's source-quality bar (first-party model cards, technical reports, arXiv, or artificialanalysis.ai) in the July 20-21 window. A full allowlist + Artificial Analysis sweep surfaced only already-ingested sources: the July 8-16 frontier-burst consolidation, GPT-5.6, Kimi K3, GLM-5.2, Muse Spark 1.1, Grok 4.5 and Inkling are all already on-page. ### Updates 2026-07-20

**Market-structure milestone — six labs now field a model above 50 on AA Intelligence Index v4.1 (up from two in early June), and near-frontier cost-per-task collapsed ~2-3x in eight days.** Artificial Analysis published a consolidation of the July 8-16 frontier burst (Grok 4.5, GPT-5.6, Muse Spark 1.1, Kimi K3 all in eight days), noting the number of labs with a model above 50 on the index has tripled from two to six [[four-frontier-launches-in-eight-days-six-labs-now-field-a-model-above-50-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-951b658c]]. The six: Anthropic (Fable 5, 60) > OpenAI (GPT-5.6 Sol max, 59) > Moonshot AI (Kimi K3, 57) > SpaceXAI (Grok 4.5 high, 54) > Z.ai (GLM-5.2 max, 51) = Meta (Muse Spark 1.1 xhigh, 51) — all already tracked here, so **no leader moves**. The genuinely new datapoint is AA's per-Intelligence-Index-task cost normalization: **Fable 5 costs $2.75/task; GPT-5.6 Sol (max) delivers within one point at $1.04/task (~1/3 the cost) and Kimi K3 within three points at $0.94/task**, while GPT-5.6 Luna (max, $0.21), Muse Spark 1.1 (xhigh, $0.26) and Grok 4.5 (high, $0.31) now all sit at or below the $0.32/task GLM-5.2 (max) set a week earlier [[four-frontier-launches-in-eight-days-six-labs-now-field-a-model-above-50-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-951b658c]]. For an agentic-coding/game-dev fan-out loop this reprices the cheap-but-capable tier: sub-$0.35/task near-frontier options now cluster where only GLM-5.2 sat a week ago. **Roster re-verification (Claude Fable 5):** the article independently re-confirms Fable 5 at AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 60, still public #1; `last_verified` bumped to 2026-07-20.

> Grok 4.5, GPT-5.6, Muse Spark 1.1, and Kimi K3 all launched within eight days. Six labs now have a model scoring above 50 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index, up from two in early June - and the price of near-frontier intelligence has collapsed. [[four-frontier-launches-in-eight-days-six-labs-now-field-a-model-above-50-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-951b658c]]

> GPT-5.6 Luna (max, $0.21), Muse Spark 1.1 (xhigh, $0.26), and Grok 4.5 (high, $0.31) all sit at or below the price GLM-5.2 (max, $0.32) set a week earlier, while Kimi K3 ($0.94) and GPT-5.6 Sol (max, $1.04) deliver within three and one points of Claude Fable 5 ($2.75) at roughly a third of its cost per task. [[four-frontier-launches-in-eight-days-six-labs-now-field-a-model-above-50-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-951b658c]]

### Updates 2026-07-19

**Kimi K3's GDPval-AA v2 Elo confirmed at 1668 — $0.94/task and native multimodal.** A new Artificial Analysis profile of Kimi K3 confirms its **GDPval-AA v2 Elo at 1668** — placing it between the prior known anchors of Opus 4.8 (1638 per the July 17 analysis) and Fable 5 (1818) on the highest-weighted v4.1 sub-evaluation [[kimi-k3-achieves-3-in-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-comparable-to-opus-4-8-and-gpt-5-5-bce8423e]]. At Kimi's $3/$15 per Mtoken pricing this translates to approximately **$0.94 per completed GDPval-AA v2 task** — significantly below Opus 4.8's estimated ~$3.12/task and far below Fable 5's ~$9+/task, making Kimi K3 a cost-attractive option for the highest-weighted agentic sub-benchmark [[kimi-k3-achieves-3-in-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-comparable-to-opus-4-8-and-gpt-5-5-bce8423e]]. The profile also confirms native multimodal input support (text, images, documents) and approximately **21% fewer output tokens per task than Kimi K2.6** — partial mitigation of K3's persistent 130M-token-per-index-task verbosity concern filed in the July 17 update [[kimi-k3-achieves-3-in-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-comparable-to-opus-4-8-and-gpt-5-5-bce8423e]]. One discrepancy: the same source reports Opus 4.8's GDPval-AA v2 Elo as **1600**, while this page's "Agentic and coding leadership" section carries **1638** from the July 17 Opus 4.8 max analysis — a 38-point gap filed as an open question below.

> Kimi K3 achieves a GDPval-AA v2 Elo of 1668. [[kimi-k3-achieves-3-in-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-comparable-to-opus-4-8-and-gpt-5-5-bce8423e]]

### Updates 2026-07-17

**Kimi K3 enters the closed-frontier cluster at AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 57, ranking 4th overall — one point above Opus 4.8 (56) — making it the highest-ranked Moonshot AI model on the index.** Moonshot AI's Kimi K3 is a proprietary 2.8-trillion-parameter model with a 1M-token context window, built for frontier software engineering, knowledge work, and deep reasoning [[kimi-k3-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-5d65f998]]. Artificial Analysis independently placed it at 57 on the AA Intelligence Index v4.1, behind Claude Fable 5 (60), GPT-5.6 Sol (max) (59), and GPT-5.6 Sol (xhigh) (58), but **above Claude Opus 4.8 (56)** — the first non-GPT-5.6 / non-Fable-5 closed model to enter the top-4 since the v4.1 index launched. The notable caveats: Kimi K3 is **extremely verbose** (130M tokens per index task, more than 2× the 63M average) and **slower than average** (62 tokens/sec vs 70 tok/sec mean), both real cost multipliers on output-dominated agentic loops [[kimi-k3-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-5d65f998]]. Pricing is $3.00/$15.00 per Mtoken — 0.6× Opus 4.8's $5/$25 but 3× Muse Spark 1.1's $1.25/$4.25. Kimi K3 is proprietary and weights are not publicly available; see [[kimi-k3]].

> Kimi K3 ranks 4th on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index at 57, behind Claude Fable 5 (60), GPT-5.6 Sol (max) (59), and GPT-5.6 Sol (xhigh) (58). [[kimi-k3-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-5d65f998]]

> Kimi K3 has 2.8 trillion parameters. It features a 1M-token context window and is designed for frontier intelligence scenarios such as software engineering, knowledge work, and deep reasoning. [[kimi-k3-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-5d65f998]]

> When evaluating the Intelligence Index, Kimi K3 generated 130M tokens, which is very verbose in comparison to the average of 63M. At 62 tokens per second, Kimi K3 is slower than average (70). [[kimi-k3-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-5d65f998]]

**Roster re-verification (Claude Fable 5):** Re-confirmed Claude Fable 5 at AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 60, still the public #1, with `last_verified` bumped to 2026-07-17 [[kimi-k3-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-5d65f998]].

### Updates 2026-07-16

**Thinking Machines ships Inkling (July 15, 2026) — its first open-weights model and the new leading US/non-Chinese open-weights model at AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 41; does NOT change the global open-weights #1 (GLM-5.2 at 51) or the top-of-board aggregate leader picture.** Mira Murati's Thinking Machines Lab released Inkling, a natively-multimodal MoE with **975B total / 41B active** parameters trained on 45T tokens of text, image, audio and video, with a **1M-token context** window (256K via the Tinker API) and open weights on Hugging Face including an NVFP4 Blackwell checkpoint [[inkling-our-open-weights-model-06531d4a]]. Artificial Analysis independently placed Inkling at **AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 41 — the new leading US open-weights model, 3 points above the prior US leader NVIDIA Nemotron 3 Ultra (38)** and well ahead of Gemma 4 31B (29) and gpt-oss-120b (24) [[thinking-machines-has-released-inkling-the-new-leading-u-s-open-weights-model-4ae0655f]]. Its most agentic-workflow-relevant property is **token efficiency**: Inkling averages **~25K output tokens per Intelligence-Index task vs 43K/38K/37K for GLM-5.2 (max), Kimi K2.6 and DeepSeek v4 Pro (max)** — a real per-task cost advantage on output-dominated agentic loops where the verbose open-weights leaders bleed budget [[thinking-machines-has-released-inkling-the-new-leading-u-s-open-weights-model-4ae0655f]]. **This does NOT move the top of the board**: at 41 it sits below the global open-weights #1 GLM-5.2 (51), below MiniMax-M3 / DeepSeek V4-Pro (44) and Kimi K2.6 (43), and far below the closed-frontier cluster (Fable 5 60 > GPT-5.6 Sol max 59 > Opus 4.8 56). Thinking Machines explicitly positions it as a customization base, not a leaderboard contender: it says Inkling is "not the strongest overall model available today, open or closed" and ships a lighter **Inkling-Small** (12B active) preview for lower-cost fine-tuning via Tinker [[inkling-our-open-weights-model-06531d4a]].

> Thinking Machines has released Inkling, the new leading U.S. open weights model, debuting at 41 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index. Inkling scores 3 points higher on the Intelligence Index (41) than the previous leading U.S. open weights model, Nemotron 3 Ultra (38), and also beats Gemma 4 31B (29) and gpt-oss-120b (24). [[thinking-machines-has-released-inkling-the-new-leading-u-s-open-weights-model-4ae0655f]]

> Inkling is token efficient compared to open weights leaders, averaging 25K output tokens per Intelligence Index task compared to 43K, 38K and 37K by GLM-5.2 (max), Kimi K2.6 and DeepSeek v4 Pro (max) respectively. [[thinking-machines-has-released-inkling-the-new-leading-u-s-open-weights-model-4ae0655f]]

> Inkling is a mixture-of-experts system with 975 billion total parameters, drawing on about 41 billion active parameters for any given task. It was trained on 45 trillion tokens of text, image, audio, and video, and reasons natively across all four. Inkling supports a context window of up to 1M tokens. [[inkling-our-open-weights-model-06531d4a]]

> Inkling is not the strongest overall model available today, open or closed. [[inkling-our-open-weights-model-06531d4a]]

### Updates 2026-07-14

**Backfill of a missed July 8 entrant — Cognition's SWE-1.7, a near-frontier agentic-coding model built by RL on top of the (already heavily RL'd) open-weight Kimi K2.7 Code base, at ~$1.97 per FrontierCode task and 1000 tokens/sec via Cerebras.** Cognition released SWE-1.7 on July 8, 2026 as the model in Devin; it is trained from a Kimi K2.7 base and the fact that large additional gains came from further RL on an already-RL-post-trained open-weight model is presented as a direct challenge to the 'post-training ceiling' thesis [[swe-1-7-frontier-intelligence-at-a-fraction-of-the-cost-0d1dcf4a]]. Vendor-reported benchmarks put SWE-1.7 at Terminal-Bench 2.1 81.5%, SWE-Bench Multilingual 77.8% (nominally edging GPT-5.5's 76.8% but behind Opus 4.8's 84.4%), and FrontierCode 1.1 Main 42.3%, with a 256K context window [[swe-1-7-frontier-intelligence-at-a-fraction-of-the-cost-0d1dcf4a]]. **This does NOT change the aggregate-intelligence leader picture** — SWE-1.7 is a coding-specialized model with no independent AA Intelligence Index placement, so Claude Fable 5 (60) > GPT-5.6 Sol max (59) > Opus 4.8 (56) all remain the aggregate frontier.

> Since SWE-1.7 was trained from a Kimi K2.7 base, which had already undergone extensive RL post-training, the large additional gains from our own training challenge the idea of a 'post-training ceiling' and suggest that RL can push capabilities much further than previously believed. [[swe-1-7-frontier-intelligence-at-a-fraction-of-the-cost-0d1dcf4a]]

> SWE-1.7 exhibits noticeably different behavior from Kimi K2.7 Code, its base model, and is significantly more aligned and trustworthy than K2.7 or other frontier open-source models. [[swe-1-7-frontier-intelligence-at-a-fraction-of-the-cost-0d1dcf4a]]

> SWE-1.7 completes a task on our FrontierCode benchmark for $1.97, available in Devin at 1000 tokens per second via Cerebras. [[swe-1-7-frontier-intelligence-at-a-fraction-of-the-cost-0d1dcf4a]]

**Roster sweep + non-events re-verified:** No roster entity's benchmark or cost field moved in the July 13-14 window. Claude Fable 5 (AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 60) and Claude Opus 4.8 (56) re-confirmed at current values against live Artificial Analysis data. **Gemini 3.5 Pro** remains pre-GA — off-allowlist reporting targets a July 17 GA after a reported rebuild but Google has confirmed neither the date, rumored 2M-token context, nor pricing.

### Updates 2026-07-13

**Meta ships Muse Spark 1.1 (July 9, 2026) — its first paid-API frontier model — AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 51 (xhigh), 1M-token context, multimodal, at aggressively low $1.25/$4.25 per Mtoken.** Meta Superintelligence Labs released Muse Spark 1.1 via the new Meta Model API (public preview), the first time Meta has offered a frontier model behind a paid API rather than gating it to first-party apps or shipping open weights [[introducing-muse-spark-1-1-f8a95609]]. It pairs a 1M-token context, full multimodal support (images, video, PDFs), built-in search with citations, and parallel tool calling — positioned as an agentic-coding foundation [[introducing-muse-spark-1-1-f8a95609]]. Artificial Analysis independently placed Muse Spark 1.1 (xhigh) at **AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 51**, up 8 points from Muse Spark 1.0's 43 in three months, **effectively tied with open-weights leader GLM-5.2 (max, 51), GPT-5.4 (xhigh) and GPT-5.6 Luna (max) at 51 — three points behind Grok 4.5 (high, 54)** [[muse-spark-1-1-meta-gains-8-intelligence-index-points-in-three-months-56cc77a0]]. **This does NOT change the top-of-board leader picture**: Claude Fable 5 (60) > GPT-5.6 Sol max (59) > Opus 4.8 (56) > GPT-5.5 (55) > Grok 4.5 high (54) all remain above it, and because Muse Spark 1.1 is proprietary (weights not released), GLM-5.2 stays open-weights #1. Its relevance is (a) a new per-lab flagship for Meta and (b) unusually aggressive economics — Meta charges $1.25/$4.25 per Mtoken (~1/4 of Anthropic/OpenAI flagship rates) at an estimated ~$0.26 per Intelligence-Index task, undercutting the cheap-but-capable tier for parallel agentic fan-out [[muse-spark-1-1-meta-gains-8-intelligence-index-points-in-three-months-56cc77a0]]. On Meta's own eval report it leads scaled/professional tool use (MCP Atlas 88.1, JobBench 54.7) but trails on contamination-resistant coding (SWE-Bench Pro 61.5 vs Opus 4.8's 69.2) — so the vendor tool-use leadership is not yet corroborated on the independent aggregate index, where it sits mid-cluster [[introducing-muse-spark-1-1-f8a95609]] [[muse-spark-1-1-meta-gains-8-intelligence-index-points-in-three-months-56cc77a0]].

> Developers can now build with Muse Spark 1.1 via the new Meta Model API, now in public preview. Muse Spark 1.1 has a context window of 1 million tokens... full multimodal support (images, video, PDFs), built-in search with citations, strong reasoning, top-tier coding abilities, structured output, and parallel tool calling. [[introducing-muse-spark-1-1-f8a95609]]

> Meta's Muse Spark 1.1 (xhigh) scores 51 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index and gains 8 points over Muse Spark 1.0 (43) in three months. It is effectively tied with GLM-5.2 (max), GPT-5.4 (xhigh), and GPT-5.6 Luna (max) at 51, three points behind Grok 4.5 (high, 54)... Meta charges $1.25 per million input tokens and $4.25 per million output tokens. [[muse-spark-1-1-meta-gains-8-intelligence-index-points-in-three-months-56cc77a0]]

> Muse Spark 1.1 achieved the highest score of 88.1 on the MCP Atlas benchmark for scaled tool use and 54.7 on JobBench for professional tool use. It scored 80.0 on Terminal-Bench 2.1, 61.5 on SWE-Bench Pro, and topped Humanity's Last Exam with 62.1. [[introducing-muse-spark-1-1-f8a95609]]

**Non-events re-verified:** **Gemini 3.5 Pro** remains in limited preview entering the second week of July — no GA, no published first-party Pro benchmarks, no final pricing, no AA Intelligence Index placement (secondary reporting still cites a 2M-token context, Deep Think reasoning, and ~$15/$60 per Mtoken, with early testers reportedly finding it trailing Fable 5 and GPT-5.6 on coding/long-horizon reasoning). No roster entity's benchmark or cost field moved this window beyond the July 8-12 re-verification.

### Updates 2026-07-10

**OpenAI ships GPT-5.6 (Sol / Terra / Luna) to GA July 9 — Sol (max) = AA Intelligence Index 59, new public #2 behind Fable 5 (60), leads AA Coding Agent Index at 80.** OpenAI moved the GPT-5.6 family from June 26 government-gated preview to full GA on July 9 across ChatGPT, Codex, and API, with pricing Sol $5/$30, Terra $2.50/$15, Luna $1/$6 per Mtoken [[gpt-5-6-frontier-intelligence-that-scales-with-your-ambition-e19fbb75]]. This retires GPT-5.5 as OpenAI's flagship. Artificial Analysis independently placed **GPT-5.6 Sol (max) at AA Intelligence Index 59 — 1 point below Fable 5 (60) at ~1/3 the cost — new public #2**, with Terra (max) 55, Luna (max) 51 [[gpt-5-6-benchmarks-across-intelligence-speed-and-cost-1b9cd635]]. **Sol (max) LEADS the AA Coding Agent Index at 80**, so aggregate-intelligence leadership (Fable 5) and coding-agent leadership (GPT-5.6 Sol) have diverged. OpenAI introduced `ultra` mode that coordinates four agents in parallel by default.

> We're launching the GPT-5.6 family of models for general availability: our new flagship Sol, alongside Terra, a balanced model for everyday work, and Luna, our most cost-efficient model. We're also introducing ultra, a new highest-capability setting that coordinates multiple agents across parallel workstreams... Ultra coordinates four agents in parallel by default. Sol is $5 input / $30 output; Terra is $2.50 input / $15 output; and Luna is $1 input / $6 output. [[gpt-5-6-frontier-intelligence-that-scales-with-your-ambition-e19fbb75]]

> GPT-5.6 Sol (max) scores 1 point below Claude Fable 5 (max) in the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index at 59 points, at approximately one third of the cost. GPT-5.6 Sol (max) leads the Artificial Analysis Coding Agent Index at 80 points. [[gpt-5-6-benchmarks-across-intelligence-speed-and-cost-1b9cd635]]

**xAI/SpaceXAI ships Grok 4.5 (July 8, 2026) — AA Intelligence Index (high) = 54, jump from Grok 4.3's 38, at $2/$6 per Mtoken; cheapest-per-task coding agent at $2.49/task.** Grok 4.5 is SpaceXAI's flagship for coding, agentic tasks, and knowledge work, trained alongside Cursor on tens of thousands of NVIDIA GB300 GPUs [[introducing-grok-4-5-c5fe4e15]]. Vendor benchmarks: Terminal-Bench 2.1 83.3%, SWE-bench Pro 64.7%, DeepSWE 1.0 62.0%. Artificial Analysis placed Grok 4.5 (high) at AA Intelligence Index 54 — below the Opus 4.8 (56) / GPT-5.5 (55) cluster and below GPT-5.6 Sol (59) on the post-GA board [[grok-4-5-brings-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-20480383]]. **Grok 4.5 in Grok Build scores 76 on the AA Coding Agent Index at just $2.49 per task** — versus Fable 5 in Claude Code ($11.80) and GPT-5.5 in Codex ($5.07) — a ~5x cost advantage at near-parity coding-agent score [[grok-4-5-brings-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-20480383]]. Co-training with Cursor and day-one free availability in Cursor deepens the model-routing-neutrality concern raised by SpaceX's $60B Anysphere/Cursor acquisition.

> Grok 4.5 is SpaceXAI's smartest model built to excel at coding, agentic tasks, and knowledge work. It was trained alongside Cursor. [[introducing-grok-4-5-c5fe4e15]]

> Grok 4.5 (high) achieves a score of 54 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index... Grok 4.5 in Grok Build scores 76 on the Artificial Analysis Coding Agent Index... Grok 4.5 in Grok Build cost $2.49 per task while Fable 5 in Claude Code cost $11.80 and GPT-5.5 in Codex $5.07. [[grok-4-5-brings-spacexai-to-the-intelligence-frontier-20480383]]

Gemini 3.5 Pro remains pre-GA (rumored ~July 17). DeepSeek V4-Pro/Flash in official rollout ramp (legacy endpoints retiring July 24) but no new first-party benchmark cleared the bar this window.

### Updates 2026-07-08

**New entrant ingested — Sakana AI's Fugu / Fugu-Ultra "orchestrator-as-a-model" (arXiv 2606.21228); vendor-reported SWE-bench Pro / Terminal-Bench 2.1 SOTA, NOT independently verified, does not change the public leader picture.** This closes the July 6 open question that had filed "Sakana Fugu Ultra" as an unverified aggregator claim: it is a real, citable entrant backed by a first-party arXiv technical report [[sakana-fugu-technical-report-a92f171d]]. Fugu is a family of orchestrator LLMs — a single foundation-model API that dynamically assembles a team of frontier models (Gemini 3.1 Pro, Claude Opus 4.8, GPT-5.5) to solve multi-step tasks, trained via large-scale fine-tuning, evolutionary algorithms, and RL. Two variants ship: Fugu (latency-balanced) and Fugu-Ultra (max quality). Fugu-Ultra reports state-of-the-art on SWE-bench Pro and Terminal-Bench 2.1 (secondary reporting puts the SWE-bench Pro figure at ~73.7%, above Opus 4.8's 69.2%). Because Fugu is a multi-agent orchestration system (not a single model) and every headline figure is vendor-reported and not independently reproduced, it does NOT displace the current public SWE-bench Pro / aggregate-intelligence leader picture; the contradiction with this page's "Opus 4.8 = best public single-model SWE-bench Pro (69.2%)" claim is filed under `## Disputes` (unresolved) and independent placement under `## Open questions`. Architecturally it is highly relevant to agentic-coding: orchestrator-as-a-model is a novel packaging of multi-agent fan-out behind one endpoint.

> Sakana Fugu models orchestrate a large pool of diverse models that includes state-of-the-art frontier LLMs such as Gemini-3.1-Pro, Claude-Opus-4.8, and GPT-5.5. [[sakana-fugu-technical-report-a92f171d]]

> Fugu-Ultra excels in agentic coding and software engineering, achieving state-of-the-art performance in both SWE Bench Pro and Terminal Bench 2.1, with a substantial leap forward in performance across both benchmarks. [[sakana-fugu-technical-report-a92f171d]]

**Leader picture re-confirmed against the live Artificial Analysis index (no change):** Claude Fable 5 (v4.1 = 60, Opus 4.8 fallback) remains the publicly-accessible aggregate-intelligence #1, ahead of Opus 4.8 (56), GPT-5.5 xhigh (55) and Sonnet 5 (53); on GDPval-AA v2, Fable 5 (1818) > Opus 4.8 (1638) > GPT-5.5 (1531); GLM-5.2 (51) holds open-weights #1 [[claude-opus-4-8-max-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-27b7d2eb]]. `last_verified` bumped on Fable 5, Opus 4.8 and GPT-5.5 accordingly.

> Claude Opus 4.8 (max, 56) is the most intelligent available model according to the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index v4.1. Claude Fable 5 (with fallback) leads at 60 but is currently unavailable; GPT-5.5 (xhigh) scores 55. [[claude-opus-4-8-max-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-27b7d2eb]]

**Non-events re-verified (checked, not asserted):** Gemini 3.5 Pro remains pre-GA — Google's early-July framing now points to a roll-out "next month" (an August slip from the prior July target); GPT-5.6 Sol/Terra/Luna remains partner-gated limited preview via API/Codex with GA still only "coming weeks" and no AA Index placement. Off-allowlist secondary reporting says the White House is finalizing its voluntary frontier-model pre-release review framework with OpenAI, Google, and Anthropic, with an announcement reportedly expected the week of July 7; no first-party source yet, filed under `## Open questions`.

### Updates 2026-07-03

**Claude Fable 5 REDEPLOYED July 1, 2026 — US export controls lifted June 30 — Fable 5 (AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 60) retakes the publicly-accessible aggregate-intelligence #1 from Opus 4.8 (56).** The US Commerce Department export controls on both Fable 5 and Mythos 5 were lifted June 30, and Fable 5 returned globally on July 1 across the Claude Platform, Claude.ai, Claude Code, and Claude Cowork [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]]. With Fable 5 accessible again at AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 60, it re-displaces Claude Opus 4.8 (56) as the effective **publicly-accessible** aggregate-intelligence leader — ending the "Opus 4.8 = effective public #1" framing that held for the June 12–30 suspension window. Availability terms: for Pro, Max, Team and select Enterprise plans, Fable 5 is included for up to 50% of weekly usage limits through July 7, then reverts to usage credits (list price unchanged at $10/$50 per Mtoken) [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]]. Anthropic's redeployment post directly undercuts the original uniqueness rationale for the ban: its testing found many less capable models could identify the same vulnerabilities and every model tested could reproduce the exploitation demonstration [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]]. Alongside redeployment, Anthropic is proposing an **industry-wide jailbreak-severity scoring framework** with Amazon, Microsoft, Google, and other Glasswing partners, distinguishing narrow (low/moderate-severity) from universal jailbreaks [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]] [[more-details-on-fable-5-s-cyber-safeguards-and-our-jailbreak-framework-aaef033d]].

> As of June 30, the export controls on Fable 5 and Mythos 5 have been lifted. Fable 5 will be available starting July 1 to users globally on the Claude Platform, Claude.ai, Claude Code, and Claude Cowork. [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]]

> Our testing confirmed that many less capable models could identify the same vulnerabilities as Fable 5, and every model tested could produce the same demonstration of vulnerability exploitation as Fable 5. We are also proposing an industry-wide framework for scoring jailbreak severity, together with Amazon, Microsoft, Google, and other Glasswing partners. [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]]

> Narrow harmful jailbreaks can elicit some specific harmful behaviors and are typically of low to moderate severity because the narrowness limits the attacker. The most concerning category is a universal jailbreak, which unblocks a wide range of harmful behaviors. [[more-details-on-fable-5-s-cyber-safeguards-and-our-jailbreak-framework-aaef033d]]

**Anthropic ships Claude Sonnet 5 (June 30, 2026) — new mid-tier frontier flagship, AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 53 (public #5), $2/$10 introductory.** Anthropic released Claude Sonnet 5, succeeding Sonnet 4.6, at an introductory $2 input / $10 output per Mtoken through August 31, 2026, reverting to $3/$15 thereafter [[introducing-claude-sonnet-5-4307222b]]. Artificial Analysis places Sonnet 5 (Adaptive Reasoning, Max Effort) at **53 on the AA Intelligence Index v4.1**, ranking public #5 behind Claude Fable 5, Opus 4.8, GPT-5.5 (xhigh) and Opus 4.7 [[claude-sonnet-5-strong-agentic-performance-at-a-higher-cost-per-task-c4346bb2]]. It is a strict improvement over Sonnet 4.6 (+9 points on Terminal-Bench v2.1) and matches or outperforms Opus 4.8 on the agentic AA-Briefcase and GDPval-AA knowledge-work evals, with early-access partners reporting it "much more agentic." Key caveat: despite a lower per-token price than Opus 4.8, Sonnet 5 costs **~$2.29 per Intelligence-Index task (~2x Sonnet 4.6, ~15% MORE than Opus 4.8)**, driven entirely by increased token usage [[claude-sonnet-5-strong-agentic-performance-at-a-higher-cost-per-task-c4346bb2]].

> Claude Sonnet 5 (Adaptive Reasoning, Max Effort) scores 53 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index, placing it behind Claude Fable 5, Claude Opus 4.8, GPT-5.5 (xhigh), and Claude Opus 4.7. [[claude-sonnet-5-strong-agentic-performance-at-a-higher-cost-per-task-c4346bb2]]

> Claude Sonnet 5 costs $2.29 per task on the Intelligence Index, a ~2x increase compared to Sonnet 4.6 and ~15% more than Claude Opus 4.8. [[claude-sonnet-5-strong-agentic-performance-at-a-higher-cost-per-task-c4346bb2]]

> Sonnet 5 is available today at an introductory price of $2 per million input tokens and $10 per million output tokens through August 31, 2026, then moves to standard pricing at $3 per million input tokens and $15 per million output tokens. [[introducing-claude-sonnet-5-4307222b]]

**Non-events re-verified:** GPT-5.6 Sol remains partner-gated with no AA Intelligence Index placement; Gemini 3.5 Pro remains pre-GA (slipped past late-June target). Neither moves the public leaderboard this window.

### Updates 2026-07-02

**Anthropic ships Claude Sonnet 5 (June 30) — a near-Opus-4.8 agentic mid-tier that is Anthropic's new cost-sensitive default, but a token-hungry one.** Anthropic released Claude Sonnet 5, its most agentic Sonnet to date, with performance Anthropic describes as similar to Opus 4.8 and improved over Sonnet 4.6 across reasoning, tool use, coding, and knowledge work; it is the default model for Free and Pro plans and ships with introductory pricing of $2/$10 per Mtoken through August 31, 2026 [[introducing-claude-sonnet-5-4307222b]]. Artificial Analysis places it at **AA Intelligence Index v4.1 = 53** (#5 overall, +6 over Sonnet 4.6), below Opus 4.8 (56) and GPT-5.5 (55) on the aggregate index, and reports **SWE-bench Verified 85.2% / SWE-bench Pro 63.2%** (Pro up from Sonnet 4.6's 58.1%) [[claude-sonnet-5-strong-agentic-performance-at-a-higher-cost-per-task-c4346bb2]]. On the agentic sub-axes it is stronger than the aggregate index suggests: it scores **GDPval-AA v2 = 1603, edging Opus 4.8 (1594 on the live leaderboard)** and sits just ahead of Opus 4.8 on AA-Briefcase as well [[claude-sonnet-5-strong-agentic-performance-at-a-higher-cost-per-task-c4346bb2]].

> Claude Sonnet 5 is our most agentic Sonnet model to date, able to make plans, use tools like browsers and terminals, and run autonomously. Sonnet 5's performance is similar to Opus 4.8, and it has improved over Sonnet 4.6 in areas including reasoning, tool use, coding, and knowledge work. [[introducing-claude-sonnet-5-4307222b]]

> Claude Sonnet 5 achieves 53 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index... Claude Opus 4.8 ranks #2 on the Intelligence Index with a score of 56, while Claude Sonnet 5 ranks #5 with a score of 53. [[claude-sonnet-5-strong-agentic-performance-at-a-higher-cost-per-task-c4346bb2]]

**The Sonnet-5 cost caveat matters for agentic fan-out: lower per-token price, HIGHER per-task cost than Opus 4.8.** Despite retaining Sonnet's $3/$15 standard pricing (vs Opus 4.8's $5/$25), Sonnet 5 costs ~$2.29 per Intelligence-Index task — ~15% MORE than Opus 4.8 — because at max effort it burns ~40% more output tokens per index task than Sonnet 4.6 and ~3x the agentic turns for GDPval-AA [[claude-sonnet-5-strong-agentic-performance-at-a-higher-cost-per-task-c4346bb2]].

> Claude Sonnet 5 costs $2.29 per task on the Intelligence Index, approximately 15% more than Claude Opus 4.8. This higher cost is driven entirely by increased token usage... With max effort, Sonnet 5 used approximately 40% more output tokens per Intelligence Index task than Sonnet 4.6, and approximately 3x the agentic turns for GDPval-AA. [[claude-sonnet-5-strong-agentic-performance-at-a-higher-cost-per-task-c4346bb2]]

**Fable 5 cloud re-enablement progresses: now GA on AWS (Amazon Bedrock + Claude Platform on AWS).** Following the July 1 global restoration, Fable 5 is now generally available on AWS with its Mythos-class capabilities and built-in safeguards, resolving part of the 'AWS/Google Cloud/Microsoft Foundry re-enablement still pending' item [[anthropic-claude-fable-5-on-aws-mythos-class-capabilities-with-built-in-safeguards-now-available-356c6653]]. Harmful cybersecurity/biology/chemistry/health prompts fall back to Opus 4.8, and a new classifier layer detects jailbreak attempts before the main model responds. Google Cloud and Microsoft Foundry re-enablement remain outstanding.

> Claude Fable 5 delivers Mythos-level capabilities available to all customers, with strong safeguards designed to make it safe for broader use... Claude Fable 5 is available on Amazon Bedrock and Claude Platform on AWS. [[anthropic-claude-fable-5-on-aws-mythos-class-capabilities-with-built-in-safeguards-now-available-356c6653]]

### Updates 2026-07-01

**Fable 5 redeployment: export controls lifted, access resumes globally.** The U.S. Department of Commerce lifted the June 12 export controls on Fable 5 and Mythos 5 as of June 30; access to Fable 5 resumed July 1 on Claude Platform, Claude.ai, Claude Code, and Claude Cowork [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]] [[commerce-department-gives-green-light-for-anthropic-to-bring-back-fable-5-e657d00f]]. **This is a leader-status change: Fable 5 (v4.1 AA Intelligence Index = 60) is once again the publicly-accessible #1**; the "Opus 4.8 as effective public #1" framing that held from June 12 through June 30 is retired. The ban was triggered by Amazon researchers finding a technique to bypass Fable 5's safeguards to identify software vulnerabilities.

> As of today, June 30, the export controls on Fable 5 and Mythos 5 have been lifted. Fable 5 will be available starting tomorrow, Wednesday, July 1, to users globally on the Claude Platform, Claude.ai, Claude Code, and Claude Cowork. [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]]

**New safety classifier with Opus 4.8 fallback.** Anthropic trained an improved safety classifier that blocks the Amazon-reported bypass technique in over 99% of cases. If a request to Fable 5 is flagged, the user is notified and the request is rerouted to Opus 4.8 instead [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]]. This is architecturally significant: the "fix" is not a capability rollback but a request-routing guard — Fable 5's base capabilities are unchanged, and only flagged requests fall back.

> Working closely with the government, we trained an improved safety classifier that targets and blocks the behavior described in the report. Users will be notified if a request to Fable 5 is blocked, and the request will instead be sent to Opus 4.8. The new classifier means that the specific technique described in the Amazon report is blocked in over 99% of cases. [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]]

**Phased access restoration.** Pro, Max, Team, and select Enterprise plans get Fable 5 for up to 50% of weekly usage limits through July 7, after which it reverts to usage-credit billing. AWS, Google Cloud, and Microsoft Foundry re-enablement is still pending [[redeploying-claude-fable-5-fdd9745e]].

### Updates 2026-06-30

**GPT-5.5 Instant — OpenAI's ChatGPT consumer default documented; 52.5% hallucination reduction in law/medicine/finance.** The first-party OpenAI announcement for GPT-5.5 Instant (default ChatGPT model for free/go tier, with personalization update June 9) is now tracked [[gpt-5-5-instant-smarter-clearer-and-more-personalized-01a8b52e]]. Key claims: 52.5% reduction in hallucinations in high-stakes domains (law, medicine, finance); 30% fewer words per response; personalization capability using search over past conversations, uploaded files, and Gmail. GPT-5.5 Instant is the ChatGPT app consumer-tier default optimized for latency — not the API reasoning model tracked elsewhere on this page. This does not change the publicly-accessible aggregate-intelligence leader: **Claude Opus 4.8 (v4.1 AA Index = 56) remains effective public #1** for API-accessible reasoning workloads.

> GPT-5.5 Instant is a generally smarter model that's more capable across everyday tasks, including improvements in analyzing photo and image uploads, answering STEM-related questions, and deciding when to use web search to provide a more useful answer. The model reduces hallucination in sensitive areas such as law, medicine, and finance by 52.5%, while maintaining the low latency of its predecessor. [[gpt-5-5-instant-smarter-clearer-and-more-personalized-01a8b52e]]

**Claude Opus 4.8 + Haiku 4.5 now GA in Microsoft Azure Foundry.** Anthropic and Microsoft announced general availability of Claude in Microsoft Foundry today [[claude-in-microsoft-foundry-is-now-generally-available-9f490039]]. Claude Opus 4.8 and Claude Haiku 4.5 are available via the Messages API with prompt caching and extended thinking, deployed Azure-natively with existing Azure identity, networking, and governance controls. This is a significant distribution milestone — Azure Foundry gives Claude access to Microsoft's enterprise customer base via familiar Azure IAM/VNet controls.

> Claude in Microsoft Foundry is generally available today. Claude Opus 4.8 and Claude Haiku 4.5 are available in the Messages API, with core capabilities like prompt caching and extended thinking to support use cases ranging from coding and agentic work to complex reasoning. Claude in Microsoft Foundry is Azure-native, working with your existing Azure identity, networking, and governance controls. [[claude-in-microsoft-foundry-is-now-generally-available-9f490039]]

**Fable 5 export suspension — Day 18; Kalshi 'before July 1' market resolving NO today.** The Kalshi market resolving 'Yes' if general access to Fable 5 is restored before July 1, 2026 had been at 57% on June 22 [[fable-5-odds-when-will-anthropic-restore-access-6f6e6af9]]; intraday June 30 odds declined to ~41.5%, indicating traders assigned less than even probability to same-day restoration. Fable 5 remains offline for all general users as of June 30 end-of-day — market resolves NO. The public-leader picture is unchanged: **Opus 4.8 = effective public #1 (v4.1 = 56)** with Fable 5 (v4.1 = 60) inaccessible.

**Gemini 3.5 Pro June GA target missed; slipped to July.** The open question filed May 27 ('Gemini 3.5 Pro: Google says ~June 2026') is partially resolved: June has passed without a Gemini 3.5 Pro GA launch. deepmind.google model listings as of June 30 show Gemini 3.5 Flash, Gemini 3.5 Audio (Live Translate), and Gemini 3.1 Pro, but no 3.5 Pro. Secondary reporting points to July 2026 GA. Public AA landscape unchanged: Google's publicly-rankable frontier remains Gemini 3.1 Pro (v4.1 = 46) plus Gemini 3.5 Flash (v4.1 = 55), with 3.5 Pro ceiling not yet benchmarkable.

### Updates 2026-06-29

**OpenAI ships GPT-5.6 (Sol / Terra / Luna) into limited preview — its new flagship, but no public AA Intelligence Index placement yet, so the public aggregate-intelligence leader is unchanged.** OpenAI announced a limited preview of the GPT-5.6 series: **Sol** (new flagship), **Terra** (balanced), and **Luna** (fast/affordable). GPT-5.6 Sol is described as OpenAI's strongest model yet, advancing the frontier on software engineering, computer use, knowledge work, scientific research, and cybersecurity, and setting a new state of the art on **Terminal-Bench 2.1** for command-line agentic workflows [[previewing-gpt-5-6-sol-a-next-generation-model-30f48121]]. Pricing is **Sol $5/$30, Terra $2.50/$15, Luna $1/$6** per Mtoken — Sol matches GPT-5.5's $5/$30 list. The series is in a limited preview to a small group of trusted partners (whose participation has been shared with the US government) via the API and Codex, with general availability in ChatGPT, Codex, and the API planned "in the coming weeks" [[previewing-gpt-5-6-sol-a-next-generation-model-30f48121]]. This resolves the standing open question about whether GPT-5.6 was delayed out of existence — it exists and is in preview. Crucially there is **no Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index placement for any GPT-5.6 variant yet** (the model is partner-gated), so this does **not** change the publicly-accessible aggregate-intelligence leader: **Claude Opus 4.8 (v4.1 AA Index = 56) remains the effective public #1** with the Mythos-class Fable 5 still inaccessible. Relevance to the agentic-coding anchor: a new Terminal-Bench-2.1-SOTA OpenAI flagship at Sol's $5/$30 (and a notably cheaper Terra tier at $2.50/$15) is a candidate driver for plan/execute/test loops once GA and independently placed.

> We're beginning a limited preview of the GPT-5.6 series: Sol, our new flagship model; Terra, a balanced model for everyday work; and Luna, a fast and affordable model. GPT-5.6 Sol is OpenAI's strongest model yet. For coding workflows, GPT-5.6 Sol sets a new state of the art on Terminal-Bench 2.1, which tests command-line workflows requiring planning, iteration, and tool coordination. [[previewing-gpt-5-6-sol-a-next-generation-model-30f48121]]

> GPT-5.6 is priced per 1M tokens across three model sizes: Sol is $5 input / $30 output; Terra is $2.50 input / $15 output; and Luna is $1 input / $6 output. We are starting with a limited preview for a small group of trusted partners and organizations, whose participation has been shared with the government, before releasing more broadly. We plan to make the GPT-5.6 family generally available in ChatGPT, Codex, and the API in the coming weeks. [[previewing-gpt-5-6-sol-a-next-generation-model-30f48121]]

**Mythos 5 partially restored for US critical-infrastructure organizations (June 26 Lutnick letter); Fable 5 remains globally suspended.** The export-control suspension has partially lifted — but only for the safeguard-free Mythos 5, not the public Fable 5. Per a June 26 letter from Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick to Anthropic co-founder Tom Brown, the export control was partially lifted to allow **Claude Mythos 5 — the unrestricted, safeguard-free model — to be deployed without an export license to a defined set of US organizations operating and defending critical infrastructure** [[claude-fable-5-still-offline-as-us-clears-mythos-5-for-critical-infrastructure-7a5ed327]]. Anthropic confirmed it was restoring access for these organizations quickly and continues to work with the government to expand Mythos 5 access and ultimately bring Fable 5 back for general use. The public safeguarded **Fable 5 — the model that was the suspended public AA Intelligence Index #1 (v4.1 = 60) — remains suspended worldwide for all general users (consumers, API developers, Claude Code, international subscribers) as of June 27, 2026, with all criminal and civil penalties from the original June 12 directive still in force** [[claude-fable-5-still-offline-as-us-clears-mythos-5-for-critical-infrastructure-7a5ed327]]. This confirms the prior open question about a Lutnick-letter Mythos-5 carve-out. It does **not** change the publicly-accessible aggregate-intelligence leader — Fable 5 is still inaccessible to the public — so **Claude Opus 4.8 remains the effective public #1**. The key model-design distinction this source clarifies: Fable 5 carries three classifier-based safety layers that redirect flagged cybersecurity, biology-chemistry, and model-distillation queries to Opus 4.8, whereas Mythos 5 removes those classifiers — which is precisely why the export-control carve-out reached only Mythos 5 (for vetted defenders) and not the broadly-available Fable 5.

> Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick partially lifted the Anthropic export control on June 26, allowing Claude Mythos 5 to be deployed without an export license to a defined set of U.S. organizations operating and defending critical infrastructure, following a June 26 letter to Anthropic co-founder Tom Brown. [[claude-fable-5-still-offline-as-us-clears-mythos-5-for-critical-infrastructure-7a5ed327]]

> The version millions of developers and subscribers had been using, Claude Fable 5, remains suspended worldwide as of June 27, 2026, with all criminal and civil penalties from the original June 12 directive still in force. The key difference between the two models is that Fable 5 includes three classifier-based safety layers that redirect flagged cybersecurity, biology-chemistry, and model-distillation queries to Claude Opus 4.8, while Mythos 5 removes those classifiers, making it more capable for both offensive and defensive cyber tasks. [[claude-fable-5-still-offline-as-us-clears-mythos-5-for-critical-infrastructure-7a5ed327]]

### Updates 2026-06-25

**Artificial Analysis launches AA-Briefcase — a multi-week agentic knowledge-work benchmark; suspended Fable 5 leads.** Artificial Analysis announced AA-Briefcase, a new frontier agentic evaluation that measures how well models perform realistic, long-horizon knowledge work across multi-week scenarios — each project has many linked tasks and thousands of input source files, with deliverables like spreadsheets, presentations, and memos graded by a combination of rubric and pairwise (analytical-quality + presentation) scoring aggregated into an AA-Briefcase Elo [[announcing-aa-briefcase-a-frontier-knowledge-work-evaluation-9a51826e]]. The (export-suspended) **Claude Fable 5 leads** the benchmark, consistent with its standing as the suspended capability ceiling — but it is also one of the highest token users at ~139k output tokens per task, and Gemini 3.5 Flash burns even more (~146k) while scoring ~720 Elo lower, reinforcing this topic's persistent verbosity-as-cost-multiplier caveat for long-horizon agentic loops [[announcing-aa-briefcase-a-frontier-knowledge-work-evaluation-9a51826e]]. This does not change the publicly-accessible leader (Opus 4.8) — Fable 5 is still inaccessible — but it adds a second multi-week agentic axis on which Fable 5 leads.

> AA-Briefcase is a new frontier agentic evaluation measuring how well AI models perform realistic, long-horizon knowledge work across multi-week scenarios. Claude Fable 5 leads the benchmark and is one of the highest token users, averaging 139k output tokens per task. Gemini 3.5 Flash uses the most output tokens of any model, averaging 146k output tokens per task, slightly more than Claude Fable 5, while scoring ~720 Elo lower. [[announcing-aa-briefcase-a-frontier-knowledge-work-evaluation-9a51826e]]

### Updates 2026-06-22

**NSA Director testimony reveals Fable 5 ban is architectural, not jailbreak-level — day 10 of suspension.** The most significant clarification yet on why the Fable 5 / Mythos 5 export-control suspension has not resolved quickly: NSA Director Gen. Joshua Rudd disclosed in a Senate Intelligence Committee briefing that Mythos, in a classified red-team exercise, autonomously broke into nearly all of the NSA's classified systems within hours [[claude-fable-5-resurfaces-in-android-app-as-nsa-breach-testimony-reshapes-ban-b8988c67]]. Senator Mark Warner quoted this testimony publicly on June 11 — one day before the June 12 ban — making the disclosure the proximate trigger of the directive. This reframes the outstanding open question on this page: the David Sacks "fix the jailbreak and the ban lifts" framing never adequately accounted for the scope of the concern; the Commerce Department's concern is with Mythos's *autonomous offensive cybersecurity capability as such*, not with a user-side exploit that Anthropic could patch.

> This is not the same as the 'jailbreak' described in earlier public accounts — it is evidence that Mythos's autonomous offensive cybersecurity capability is itself the national security concern, not a user-side exploit that can be patched. The revelation explains why David Sacks's 'fix the jailbreak and the ban lifts' framing has not translated into a quick resolution — the underlying concern is architectural, not prompt-level. [[claude-fable-5-resurfaces-in-android-app-as-nsa-breach-testimony-reshapes-ban-b8988c67]]

**Fable 5 name reappears in Android app selector but access is still blocked.** On June 21, 2026 developers reported that the name "Claude Fable 5" had reappeared in the model selector inside the Claude Android app; attempts to invoke the model produced an error message reading "server is temporarily rate-limiting requests" — a changed message from earlier in the suspension but not an actual restoration [[claude-fable-5-resurfaces-in-android-app-as-nsa-breach-testimony-reshapes-ban-b8988c67]].

> Fable 5 is not back as of June 21, 2026. Developers reported that the name 'Claude Fable 5' had reappeared in the model selector inside the Claude Android app, but attempts to invoke the model produced a changed error message reading 'server is temporarily rate-limiting requests.' [[claude-fable-5-resurfaces-in-android-app-as-nsa-breach-testimony-reshapes-ban-b8988c67]]

**Prediction markets price ~57–75% chance of restoration before July.** On the Kalshi prediction market, traders price a 57% chance restoration will occur before July 1 (two weeks out from the date), 67% before July 10, and 75% before July 17 [[fable-5-odds-when-will-anthropic-restore-access-6f6e6af9]]. Polymarket shows a parallel market with "July 1" as the leading outcome at 55%, followed by "June 26" at 28% [[fable-5-odds-when-will-anthropic-restore-access-6f6e6af9]]. These figures are consistent with Anthropic's MD's earlier "very confident in coming days" framing [[anthropic-says-mythos-and-fable-5-access-could-return-in-coming-days-after-white-house-block-0185d598]], but the NSA testimony context (architectural capability concern) argues against a near-term resolution unless Anthropic implements hard routing/geo-fencing satisfactory to Commerce.

> On the Kalshi prediction market for when Anthropic will restore Fable 5 access, traders price a 57% chance the restoration will occur before July 1, which is two weeks away. There's also a 67% chance access is restored before July 10, and a 75% chance it happens before July 17. [[fable-5-odds-when-will-anthropic-restore-access-6f6e6af9]]

**The v4.1 category weighting is now explicit: Agents 34% / Coding 24% / Scientific Reasoning 24% / General 18%** [[claude-opus-4-8-max-intelligence-performance-price-analysis-27b7d2eb]]. With agentic + coding tasks now 58% of the aggregate index, the v4.1 leaderboard is materially more representative of agentic-coding workflows than v4.0 was.

> Intelligence Index v4.1 is calculated as a weighted average across four categories: Agents (34%), Coding (24%), Scientific Reasoning (24%) and General (18%), with the weighting emphasizing agentic tasks.

No new public model shipped in this window and the publicly-accessible leader is unchanged (Opus 4.8). Today's event is a measurement re-placement that completes the v4.0→v4.1 migration for the closed-frontier tier. Open caveat carried forward: Gemini 3.1 Pro and Grok 4.3 still need their own v4.1 re-placements.

### Updates 2026-06-17

**Artificial Analysis migrated the Intelligence Index to v4.1 — an agentic-workloads reweighting that re-scales every model's score, so cross-version comparisons must be made carefully.** v4.1 makes three changes: it updates and reweights evaluations toward agentic tasks (Terminal-Bench Hard → Terminal-Bench 2.1, τ²-Bench Telecom → τ³-Bench Banking), upgrades GDPval-AA to **GDPval-AA v2** (now the single highest-weighted evaluation in the index), and removes IFBench for saturation [[artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-v4-1-a-shift-toward-agentic-workloads-e0bce552]]. GDPval-AA v2 re-baselines Elo to human expert performance at 1000, swaps to a rotating panel of frontier-model judges, and raises the agent turn limit from 100 to 250 for longer-horizon trajectories [[artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-v4-1-a-shift-toward-agentic-workloads-e0bce552]].

> v4.1 marks a broader shift toward agentic workloads, with three main changes: updated and reweighted evaluations toward agentic tasks, including moving Terminal-Bench Hard to Terminal-Bench 2.1 and τ²-Bench Telecom to τ³-Bench Banking; upgrading GDPval-AA to GDPval-AA v2; and removing IFBench due to saturation. GDPval-AA v2 is the highest weighted evaluation in the Intelligence Index v4.1.

The re-scale is large and matters for this topic's leaderboard: under v4.1 the same models score materially differently than on the v4.0 numbers this page currently quotes — e.g. Kimi K2.6 moves from 54 (v4.0) to **43** (v4.1) and GLM-5.1 from 51 (v4.0) to **40** (v4.1) [[glm-5-2-is-the-new-leading-open-weights-model-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-ef3ea3a0]]. v4.0 and v4.1 AA Intelligence Index numbers are therefore **not directly comparable**; the entity-page `aa_intelligence_index` fields and the Summary's leader figures predate v4.1 and need a version qualifier on the next sweep.

**GLM-5.2 takes the open-weights #1 slot on the new v4.1 basis.** Z.ai's GLM-5.2 — same 744B-total / 40B-active MoE size as GLM-5.1, 1M-token context — now has an independent AA placement of **51 on Intelligence Index v4.1**, 11 points above GLM-5.1's v4.1 score, ahead of MiniMax-M3 (44), DeepSeek V4-Pro max (44) and Kimi K2.6 (43) [[glm-5-2-is-the-new-leading-open-weights-model-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-ef3ea3a0]]. The gains concentrate in scientific reasoning (CritPt +16 to 21%, HLE +12 to 40%), long-context reasoning (AA-LCR +9 to 71%), agentic banking (τ³ banking +15 to 27%) and SciCode (+7 to 50%); first-party API pricing holds in line with GLM-5.1 at $1.4/$4.4/$0.26 per 1M input/output/cache-hit tokens [[glm-5-2-is-the-new-leading-open-weights-model-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-ef3ea3a0]]. The verbosity caveat persists: GLM-5.2 burns 43k output tokens per index task (37k reasoning), up from GLM-5.1's 26k and well above MiniMax-M3 (24k) — a real cost multiplier on output-dominated agentic loops [[glm-5-2-is-the-new-leading-open-weights-model-on-the-artificial-analysis-intelligence-index-ef3ea3a0]]. This supersedes the prior open-weights framing (MiniMax-M3 55 / Kimi-K2.6 54 on v4.0), which was measured on the now-superseded index version. See [[glm-5]].

> Z.ai's GLM-5.2 is the new leading open weights model on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index, scoring 51. GLM-5.2 is the same size as GLM-5.1 (744B total / 40B active parameters) but scores 11 points higher on the Intelligence Index v4.1, placing ahead of MiniMax-M3 (44), DeepSeek V4 Pro (max, 44) and Kimi K2.6 (43).

**Grok V9-Medium began public release June 16, 2026 — but with no independent benchmarks and an acquisition-driven conflict-of-interest wrinkle.** xAI's 1.5T-parameter (3× the ~500B v8-small) coding model, trained explicitly on licensed Cursor developer-workflow sessions, started rolling out publicly on the same morning SpaceX signed a definitive $60B agreement to acquire Anysphere (Cursor) [[grok-v9-medium-arrives-as-spacex-seals-cursor-developers-face-model-choice-risk-37bb8bd7]]. xAI has telegraphed it will benchmark V9-Medium against Claude and GPT-5.5 on SWE-bench Verified — where current public references put Opus 4.6 and Gemini 3.1 Pro at ~80-81%, GPT-5.5 at 88.7%, and the prior Grok 4 series at ~72-75% — but no V9-Medium scores have been published or independently placed yet [[grok-v9-medium-arrives-as-spacex-seals-cursor-developers-face-model-choice-risk-37bb8bd7]]. Relevance to the agentic-coding anchor: a frontier lab acquiring the multi-model IDE its own model is trained to compete inside is a model-routing-neutrality risk worth tracking. No public leaderboard leader change from V9-Medium pending independent benchmarks.

> Grok V9-Medium is a 1.5-trillion-parameter coding model trained explicitly on Cursor developer workflows, three times the size of the current production model v8-small at approximately 500 billion parameters, with its public release beginning in mid-June 2026.

> SpaceX signed a definitive $60 billion merger agreement to acquire Anysphere, the company behind AI coding tool Cursor, on the same morning that Grok V9-Medium's public release began.

No change to the publicly-accessible aggregate-intelligence leader (Claude Opus 4.8 remains #1 public after the Fable 5 / Mythos 5 export-control suspension). The dominant story today is the v4.1 index re-scale, which is a measurement-methodology event, not a capability change.

### Updates 2026-06-16

**FrontierMath v2 audit (Epoch AI, June 12, 2026) reshuffles math-reasoning scores upward — a benchmark-integrity story, not a capability story.** Epoch released FrontierMath: Tiers 1-4 (v2), concluding an audit that corrected errors in **42% of problems**; rankings stayed similar but scores rose sharply across the board [[frontiermath-tier-4-v2-9558ff2f]]. The corrected dataset is now 338 problems (295 in Tiers 1-3, 43 in the hardest Tier 4); the v2 release corrected 123 Tier 1-3 problems and 12 Tier 4 problems while dropping 5 and 7 respectively [[frontiermath-tier-4-v2-9558ff2f]]. The magnitude of the artifact is striking: GPT-5.5 (xhigh)'s **Tier 4 score jumped from 35% to 73%** purely from fixing flawed reference answers — a caution that even a flagship contamination-resistant math benchmark was substantially mis-scored before audit [[frontiermath-tier-4-v2-9558ff2f]].

> FrontierMath: Tiers 1-4 (v2) is live. We concluded an audit that addressed errors in 42% of problems. Rankings are similar but scores are higher across the board. The current leaders are GPT-5.5 (xhigh) with 85% on Tiers 1-3 and Google's AI co-mathematician with 76% on Tier 4.

> GPT-5.5-xhigh's FrontierMath 4 score jumped from 35% to 73% after EpochAI fixed errors in the benchmark.

**Claude Fable 5 tops the audited FrontierMath axis**, reinforcing (but not changing) its standing as the suspended capability leader. Post-audit, Fable 5 hits **87% on Tiers 1-3 and 88% on the hardest Tier 4 (v2)** — roughly **13 points ahead of GPT-5.5 (~75%)** on FrontierMath's toughest problems, one of the hardest math-reasoning benchmarks in the AA-adjacent suite [[claude-fable-5-outpaces-gpt-5-5-by-13-points-on-frontiermath-s-toughest-problems-b1737496]]. This is a new contamination-audited axis on which the (currently US-export-suspended) Fable 5 leads, consistent with its existing #1 standing on the AA Intelligence Index; it does not move the *publicly accessible* leader, which remains Claude Opus 4.8.

> According to Epoch AI, Fable 5 hits 87 percent accuracy on tiers 1 through 3 and 88 percent on the hardest tier 4 (v2). OpenAI's GPT-5.5 reaches about 75 percent on the same tier, well behind Fable 5, which represents approximately a 13-point difference in performance on FrontierMath's toughest problems.

No leader changes and no new public models shipped in this window: Grok V9-Medium remains pre-public-API as of June 16 (still no independent benchmarks), and Gemini 3.5 Pro remains in limited Vertex preview pending GA. Relevance to the user's anchor domains: the 42%-error-rate finding is a concrete data point for the topic's standing benchmark-integrity caveat — vendor and even third-party benchmark numbers quoted in agentic-coding model selection should be treated as provisional until audited.

### Updates 2026-06-15

Z.AI released **GLM-5.2**, the latest model in the tracked GLM-5 open-weights line. It launched June 13, 2026 on the GLM Coding Plan tiers, with the standalone API, Z.ai chatbot, and MIT open-weights release confirmed for the following week [[zhipu-ai-open-sources-glm-5-2-with-1-million-token-context-cbc95c1c]]. (Distinct from the June 13 log entry, which covered the US-directive access suspension; this entry covers the model itself.) GLM-5.2 keeps GLM-5's 744B-total / 40B-active MoE architecture but adds a usable 1M-token context window — among the largest from any open-weights LLM — and a new dual thinking-effort system (High / Max), with 131,072 max output tokens; Z.AI recommends Max as the default for coding [[zhipu-ai-open-sources-glm-5-2-with-1-million-token-context-cbc95c1c]].

> GLM-5.2 is built on the same 744-billion-parameter Mixture-of-Experts architecture as GLM-5, with a usable 1-million-token context window and a new dual thinking-effort system (High and Max).

No benchmark numbers were published at launch; vendor coding/long-horizon claims are unverified pending independent measurement [[zhipu-ai-open-sources-glm-5-2-with-1-million-token-context-cbc95c1c]]. No leader changes: the AA Intelligence Index top order is unchanged (Claude Fable 5 ~65, Opus 4.8 at 61, GPT-5.5 at 60; Kimi K2.6 still leads open weights at 54). The 1M-context window is the practically notable axis for agentic-coding workflows (full PR-scale diffs + long plan-then-execute traces) and for game-dev codebase ingestion.

> Zhipu did not publish benchmark numbers at launch; vendor claims (powerful coding, strong long-horizon) are unverified for now.

_no contradictions or gaps known yet (last reviewed: 2026-06-15)_

### Updates 2026-06-14

**Google ships Gemini 3.5 Live Translate** — a streaming speech-to-speech audio model rolling out June 13 across the Gemini Live API, Google Meet, and the Google Translate app on Android/iOS [[fluid-natural-voice-translation-with-gemini-3-5-live-translate-5c65331b]]. Unlike sentence-buffered translation, it translates incrementally while the speaker is still talking and preserves the original speaker's vocal characteristics, auto-detecting 70+ languages without manual language selection.

> Gemini 3.5 Live Translate operates as a streaming model, beginning to translate while the speaker is still talking and staying only a few seconds behind to maintain context and conversational flow.

Methodologically it is a narrow, distilled variant rather than a new frontier intelligence tier: the model card states it is **based on Gemini 3 Pro** with a 128K audio-input / 64K output window and is explicitly **less capable than Gemini 3.1 Pro** — it does not move the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index leader picture [[gemini-3-5-audio-live-translate-model-card-68bc7af3]]. Google's frontier-intelligence tier remains the suspended Fable 5 (#1, inaccessible) and Claude Opus 4.8 (#2, public leader).

> Gemini 3.5 Live Translate is based on Gemini 3 Pro. Input: Audio with a token context window of up to 128K. Output: Audio and text, with up to 64K token output. Gemini 3.5 Live Translate is less capable than Gemini 3.1 Pro.
